(7)\n\nEquations (5)-(7) are carried as part of the input, with the full joint\nproof appended. The quantitative exclusion below does not require input\ninterpolation feasibility, these input values, or the particular\nminimizer in (6). It depends only on the explicit vectors (4), and\nallows all values and the common minimizer to be reselected.\n\n## D2. The local motifs retained by the entire current child\n\nPut v=G_4 and set\n\n    u_left=Y_31, u_right=Y_7, c_dep=sigma r_0,\n    c_parallel=(K-1)/rho.\n\nEvery assertion in the following list follows from the defining formulas (2)-(4), since the\nlater containing maps are similarities and have zero relative translation:\n\n    ||P||=R, ||v||^2=k,\n    Y_3=Y_27=0,\n    u_right=-delta r_0 G_3,\n    u_left=-delta r_0 G_27,\n    Y_30-u_left=c_parallel w,\n    P=mG_0+aG_2+G_3,\n    c_dep G_3+mG_4+aG_6+G_7=0,\n    c_dep G_27+mG_28+aG_30+w=0.                    (8)\n\nFor the dependency, the first internal bridge is\nt+alpha Tp=-r_0 Lw_atom, while alpha Tp=mG_4+aG_6+G_7\nand t=-delta r_0 G_3. Moving terms gives its coefficient\nc_dep=(1-delta)r_0. The last seven-step piece is an exact scaled\ncopy, so the identical calculation gives the last dependency.\n\nThere are isometric atom maps J_first,J_last with\n\n    (G_4,G_6,G_7,u_right)\n       =alpha J_first(U,V,w_atom,chi p),\n    (G_28,G_30,w,u_left)\n       =gamma J_last(U,V,w_atom,chi p).              (9)\n\nThe signs and scales in (9) follow from t=chi alpha Tp. In particular\nG_27=-gamma J_last p/c_dep. Define the nonnegative anchor price\n\n    Danchor=chi(D+R^2/c_dep).                        (10)\n\nThen\n\n    <w-G_27,u_left>=gamma^2 Danchor,\n    <G_7-G_3,u_right>=k Danchor.                     (11)\n\nBoth quantities vanish at sigma=1 and must be retained otherwise.\nThey replace the false equality of old zero-residual anchor heights.\n\nFor the residual-shifted right initial vector define\n\n    Pshift=P-u_right=P+delta r_0 G_3,\n    Rshift^2=||Pshift||^2\n            =R^2+2delta r_0 D+delta^2 r_0^2 N,\n    Dshift=-<Pshift,v>\n          =m(sigma r_0 D/R^2+chi k)>0.              (12)\n\nIndeed <P,v>=-sigma r_0 Dm/R^2 by the first cross identity, whereas\n<u_right,v>=chi k m. Thus the adverse signed correlation is changed\nand explicitly paid; it is not copied from a zero-residual child.\n\n## D3. The full released affine model and its normalization\n\nTake arbitrary similarities E,F from R^9 into any common Euclidean\nspace, with E^T E=ell I_9,F^T F=nu I_9 and ell,nu>=0. Define\n\n    t_join=E(u_left-rw)-FP.\n\nThe actual parent samples have\n\n    X_i=EZ_i, g_i=EG_i,                                  i=0..31,\n    X_(32+j)=t_join+FZ_j, g_(32+j)=FG_j,                  j=0..30,\n    X_63=t_join+FZ_31, g_63=g_free.\n\nThe bridge X_32=E(u_left-rw) is exactly the step 1+r from X_31.\nAll other queried updates follow from the actual child word. All\nparent values f_parent,i are free. Add one free common optimum\n(z_opt,0,0). Impose every distinct original ordered row\n\n    Q_ij=f_parent,i-f_parent,j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>\n                                      +||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.      (13)\n\nThere are 4160 such rows on {star,0,...,63}. A Gram description uses\nthe 9 columns of E, 9 columns of F, z_opt and g_free. Its diagonal\nframe blocks are ell I_9 and nu I_9; ALL other blocks are free and the\nENTIRE twenty-by-twenty Gram is PSD. In particular every singular\nblock range condition is retained. This is equivalent to the\nEuclidean formulation above by Gram factorization.\n\nNormalize by an actual common-minimizer distance ||EP-z_opt||, or by\nthe nearest-minimizer distance of the resulting static interpolant\nwhen constructing a witness. The family's free scale is closed under\nrescaling to distance one. No inherited surrogate radius replaces\nthat choice. Necessity of (13) follows from accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290`; sufficiency would give ONE static convex smooth\nfunction for the entire run. Our contradiction arises entirely in\nthe queried rows, before either normalization or the optimum is used.\n\n## R1. Quantitative statement in the complete original model\n\nFor the exact vectors of D1-D3, with ANY sigma in [9999/10000,1],\nANY similarities E,F with squared scales ell>0 and nu>=0, and ANY\nreal values at the queried samples, set\n\n    E_rec=ell gamma^2 e>0,\n    I_loc={27,28,...,39}.\n\nThe following is the first new candidate conclusion:\n\n    max_{i,j in I_loc, i!=j} Q_ij > E_rec/10000.       (R1)\n\nThere is no restriction on the common optimum, terminal gradient,\ndimension, full cross Gram, or relative translation beyond the physical\nbridge in D3. The statement is stronger than exact infeasibility of\nthis fixed geometry: even the optimal choice of all values cannot make\nall these queried residuals arbitrarily small relative to E_rec.\nIt is not an upper bound on W_63 and does not cover arbitrary child\ngeometries. All original rows of D3 remain in the model.\n\nWe prove R1 by assuming Q_ij<=b_err on these pairs, where\n\n    0<=epsilon<=1/10000, b_err=epsilon E_rec,\n\nand deriving a contradiction. All error charges below are explicit.\nThe proof also records the precise changed-state component needed\nwhen the fixed geometry survives only as an orthogonal projection.\n\n## R2. Coordinate shift and priced original paths\n\nSubtract E u_left from all positions, and describe the right intrinsic\ncoordinates relative to u_right. The physical relative translation is\n\n    tbar=-r Ew-F Pshift.\n\nDefine heights from the arbitrary, freely selected parent values by\n\n    hL_i=f_parent,i-<EG_i,E(Z_i-u_left)>+ell||G_i||^2/2,\n    hR_j=f_parent,32+j-<FG_j,F(Z_j-u_right)>+nu||G_j||^2/2.\n\nThe right height excludes tbar. Expansion of the original Q gives\n\n    Q_(i,k)=hL_i-hL_k+ell<G_i-G_k,Y_k-u_left>,\n    Q_(32+j,32+l)=hR_j-hR_l+nu<G_j-G_l,Y_l-u_right>,\n    Q_(i,32+j)=hL_i-hR_j+<EG_i,tbar>\n                         +<EG_i-FG_j,F(Y_j-u_right)>,\n    Q_(32+j,i)=hR_j-hL_i-<FG_j,tbar>\n                         +<FG_j-EG_i,E(Y_i-u_left)>.\n                                                            (R2)\n\nPut H_L=hL_31, H_R=hR_7, DeltaH=H_R-H_L. In these shifted\ncoordinates the receiving residuals at left31 and right7 vanish.\nThe earlier anchors27 and right3 have nonzero residuals, and the\nopposite original rows between these two pairs give\n\n    H_L-ell gamma^2 Danchor-b_err <= hL_27 <= H_L+b_err,\n    H_R-nu k Danchor-b_err <= hR_3 <= H_R+b_err.       (R3)\n\nThe actual reverse paths 31->30->29->28 and 39->38->37->36 have\nnormals -ell gamma^2 d_atom,0,0 and -nu k d_atom,0,0,\nrespectively. Therefore the following uniform lower bounds hold:\n\n    hL_28,hL_30 >= H_L-ell gamma^2 d_atom-3b_err,\n    hR_4,hR_6 >= H_R-nu k d_atom-3b_err.             (R4)\n\nThe path 35->34->33->32 is in the first right atom. Its shift by\n-u_right contributes telescoping gradient differences. For j=0,2,\n\n    hR_j >= hR_3-nu d_atom\n                   +nu<G_j-G_3,u_right>-3b_err.     (R5)\n\nThese identities can be checked directly: the first edge of each\nunshifted atomic reverse path has normal\n<w_atom-V,y_2>=-d_atom; the next has\n<V-U,y_1>=0; the final edge has equal U gradients. Shifting a\nreceiving residual by -u_right adds\n-<G_source-G_receiver,u_right> to that normal. Summing the three\nheight inequalities gives exactly R5. No free value has been fixed.\n\nIntroduce the nonnegative prices and coefficients\n\n    Cdep=A+c_dep, Kdep=Cdep-1,\n    Jprice=(A-1)d_atom+c_dep Danchor,\n    Vprice=(a d_atom+c_dep Danchor)/m,\n    Aplus=A+delta r_0,\n    Bprice=(A-1)d_atom+Aplus k Danchor,\n    hcoef=(a+1+c_dep)/m.\n                                                            (R6)\n\nThe cross rows into the two zero-residual receiving anchors give\n\n    <EG_i,tbar> <= H_R-hL_i+b_err,\n    <FG_j,tbar> >= hR_j-H_L-b_err.                   (R7)\n\nThe last-left dependency and the first-right dependency in D2 have\npositive coefficients c_dep,m,a,1 and total Cdep. Multiply R7 by\nthese coefficients and insert R3-R4. The weighted height-error\ncoefficient is Cdep+c_dep+3(A-1)=4A+2c_dep-3. Since this is\nless than 4Cdep, one obtains\n\n    -ell gamma^2 Jprice/Cdep-4b_err <= DeltaH\n                      <= nu k Jprice/Cdep+4b_err.    (R8)\n\nUse -w=c_dep G_27+mG_28+aG_30 in the first half of R7.\nThe error before inserting R8 is\n[2c_dep+4(A-1)]b_err<25b_err. With Kdep<8 this yields\n\n    <Ew,tbar> >= -Kdep DeltaH-ell gamma^2 Jprice-25b_err\n       >= -Kdep nu k Jprice/Cdep-ell gamma^2 Jprice-57b_err.\n                                                            (R9)\n\nUse -m v=aG_6+G_7+c_dep G_3 in the second half of R7.\nIts extra error is [(4a+1+2c_dep)/m]b_err<4b_err. Since\nhcoef<3/2, insertion of the lower bound in R8 gives\n\n    <Fv,tbar> <= -hcoef DeltaH+nu k Vprice+4b_err\n       <= hcoef ell gamma^2 Jprice/Cdep+nu k Vprice+10b_err.\n                                                            (R10)\n\nFinally Pshift=mG_0+aG_2+(1+delta r_0)G_3. Apply R5,R3,R7\nwith these positive coefficients. The residual term in the weighted\nsum is\n\n    nu<P-A G_3,u_right>=nu delta r_0(A N-D)>=0.\n\nThe bound A N>D is established below. The error coefficient is\n5(m+a)+2(1+delta r_0)<28. Dropping the displayed nonnegative\nterm gives\n\n    <F Pshift,tbar> >= Aplus DeltaH-nu Bprice-28b_err. (R11)\n\nThis is where the old zero-residual simplification would lose both\nthe true anchor price and the new error charges. Every row used\nabove has both global indices in I_loc.\n\n## R3. Elementary bounds from the defining parameters\n\nSquaring positive rationals gives\n14142/10000<sqrt(2)<14143/10000. Substitution in D1 yields\n\n    7/5<a<3/2, 341/100<m<171/50,\n    241/100<K<121/50, 1<z<201/200,\n    29/5<A<6, 12/5<r_0<49/20<5/2,\n    14<R^2<71/5, 2/5<N<21/50,\n    99/100<D<11/10, 1/2<e<3/5,\n    rho>99/100, 0<=chi<=1/9999, delta<=1/10000.       (R12)\n\nFor completeness, R^2=m^2+Kz lies between\n(341/100)^2+241/100 and\n(171/50)^2+(121/50)(201/200)=14.1285<71/5.\nHere the terminating decimal is an exact rational expression.\nFor N=rho^2 z/K, use rho^2>99/100 for its lower bound;\n(99/100)/(121/50)>2/5. Its upper bound follows from\n(201/200)/(241/100)<21/50. For e=z(1-1/K), the two bounds\nfollow from 1-100/241>1/2 and (201/200)(71/121)<3/5.\n\nConsequently\n\n    239/100<c_dep<5/2, Cdep>8, Kdep<8,\n    0<k<1/5, 841/25<r<36,\n    0<c_parallel<3/2, 1+c_parallel/r<21/20,\n    hcoef<3/2, Aplus<61/10,\n    0<=Danchor<1/1400,\n    0<Jprice/e<1/84, 0<Jprice<1/140,\n    0<=Vprice/e<1/560, 0<Bprice<3/500,\n    14<Rshift^2<71/5, Dshift/Rshift^2>1/25.           (R13)\n\nHere k<(5/2)^2(21/50)/14<1/5. The bridge bounds follow\nfrom lambda^2>29/5, lambda^2<6, and 29/5<A<6. The\nc_parallel and hcoef bounds follow from R12 and rho>99/100.\nSince R^2/c_dep<(71/5)/(239/100)<6,\nDanchor<(71/10)/9999<1/1400 and Danchor/e<1/700. Thus\n\n    Jprice/e<5/600+(5/2)/700=1/84,\n    Vprice/e<[(3/2)/600+(5/2)/700]/(17/5)=1/560,\n    Bprice<5/1000+(61/10)(1/5)(1/1400)<3/500.\n\nThe extra terms in Rshift^2 beyond R^2 are at most\n2(1/10000)(5/2)(11/10)+(1/10000)^2(5/2)^2(21/50).\nAdding this to 14.1285 still gives less than 71/5. From D2,\n\n    Dshift/Rshift^2 >= sigma r_0 Dm/(R^2 Rshift^2)\n       >[(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)(341/100)]/(71/5)^2>1/25.\n\nFor the last comparison the numerator is the exact rational\n8.09405784>8.0656=(71/5)^2/25. The discarded chi k term is\nnonnegative. Also A N>(29/5)(2/5)>11/10>D, proving the sign\nused in R11. Finally delta r_0<1/4000 and m+a=A-1<5\nprove the error bound 5(m+a)+2(1+delta r_0)<28.\n\n## R4. Independent scales remain controlled despite row error\n\nThe bounds R9,R10,R13 imply\n\n    <Ew,tbar> >= -nu e/420-ell gamma^2 Jprice-57b_err,\n    <Fv,tbar> <= (3/16)ell gamma^2 Jprice+nu e/2800+10b_err.\n\nMultiply the second bound by 1+c_parallel/r<21/20. Its right\nside is nonnegative, so this replacement of the positive multiplier\nis valid even if the actual inner product is negative. Subtraction\ngives\n\n    <Ew,tbar>-(1+c_parallel/r)<Fv,tbar>\n       >= -nu e/360-ell gamma^2 e/70-68b_err.         (R14)\n\nThe numerical constants are exact rational comparisons:\n1/420+(21/20)/2800=463/168000<1/360,\n[1+(21/20)(3/16)]/84=383/26880<1/70, and\n57+(21/20)10=135/2<68.\n\nSet T_aff=nu/(ell gamma^2). The physical identity\n\n    nu Rshift^2=r^2 ell gamma^2 N+2r<Ew,tbar>+||tbar||^2\n\nand R9 imply\n\n    T_aff >= [r^2 N-2r Jprice-114r epsilon e]\n                  /[Rshift^2+2r Kdep k Jprice/Cdep]. (R15)\n\nFrom R13,\n\n    2Jprice/(rN)<1/800,\n    2r Kdep k Jprice/(Cdep Rshift^2)<9/1225<3/400,\n    114e/(rN)<6.\n\nSince epsilon<=1/10000,\n\n    T_aff > [(1-1/800-6/10000)/(1+3/400)]\n                                            r^2 N/Rshift^2\n          > (99/100)r^2 N/Rshift^2>0.               (R16)\n\nThe last strict comparison is\n0.99815>0.99(1.0075)=0.997425, written as exact rationals.\nIn particular nu=0 cannot satisfy the assumed approximate rows.\n\nThe second norm identity\n||F Pshift+tbar||^2=r^2 ell gamma^2 N, together with R11\nand the lower bound in R8, gives\n\n    T_aff <= [r^2 N+2Aplus Jprice/Cdep\n                            +(8Aplus+56)epsilon e]\n                   /[Rshift^2-2Bprice] <40.          (R17)\n\nIndeed 8Aplus+56<105; the numerator without the last term is\nless than 545; the last term is less than 105(3/5)/10000<1;\nand the denominator is greater than 139/10. Hence the ratio is\nless than 5460/139<40. Both bounds use full Euclidean norm\nidentities, including every PSD range condition. Neither scale nor\nthe top translation was fixed by the proof.\n\n## R5. The original six-cycle still has a fixed positive margin\n\nLet S_cycle be the sum of the six ORIGINAL rows\n\n    Q_(36,30)+Q_(30,31)+Q_(31,39)\n                   +Q_(39,38)+Q_(38,37)+Q_(37,36).\n\nAll free values cancel. Use R2, the actual atomic reverse path,\nY_30-u_left=c_parallel w, and r Ew=-tbar-F Pshift to obtain\nthe exact identity\n\n    S_cycle=(c_parallel Dshift/r)nu-ell gamma^2 e\n             +<Ew,tbar>-(1+c_parallel/r)<Fv,tbar>\n             -nu k d_atom.                          (R18)\n\nFor clarity, before eliminating Ew in the first cross term the\nsame sum is\n<Fv,E(gamma J_last y_2)>-ell gamma^2 e\n+<Ew-Fv,tbar>-nu k d_atom. The substitution in R18 uses\ngamma J_last y_2=c_parallel w and <v,Pshift>=-Dshift.\nNo approximate row bound enters this algebraic identity.\n\nSince c_parallel N/e=rho, R13 and R16 give\n\n    [(c_parallel Dshift/r)nu]/E_rec\n       >(99/100)r rho Dshift/Rshift^2\n       >(99/100)^2(841/25)(1/25)>131/100.\n\nUse R14,R17 and k d_atom/e<1/3000 in R18. Then\n\n    S_cycle/E_rec\n       >31/100-40(1/360+1/3000)-1/70-68epsilon\n       >=31/100-28/225-1/70-68/10000\n       >4/25.                                      (R19)\n\nThe last comparison is equivalent to\n1079/6300-17/2500>4/25; after multiplying by the positive\ndenominator 6300*2500 the excess numerator is 70400.\nOn the other hand the assumed six row bounds imply\nS_cycle/E_rec<=6epsilon<=6/10000<4/25. This proves R1.\n\nAt ell=0 the scale-normalized R1 is not asserted. Exact feasibility\nstill forces nu=0: X_31=X_32=0, g_31=0, and the sum of the two\nopposite original rows is ||FG_0||^2=nu. The quantitative claim is\nexplicitly about the nonzero left-scale face.\n\n## E1. A necessary new gradient-energy component\n\nSuppose now that an ACTUAL full static convex 1-smooth trajectory on\nh_63 is realized in a larger Euclidean space. Suppose its projection\nonto one orthogonal summand is precisely a D3 transcript with ell>0.\nWrite the full points and gradients as\n\n    Xhat_i=(X_i,z_i), ghat_i=(g_i,v_i),\n    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i v_i.\n\nThe extra component need not itself be the gradient of a separate\nfunction. Every full value is assigned to the projected Q when making\nthe following algebraic decomposition:\n\n    Qhat_ij=Qbase_ij+Qperp_ij,\n    Qperp_ij=<v_i,z_j-z_i>+||v_i-v_j||^2/2.           (E1)\n\nThe local step sum is exactly\n\n    Hloc=sum_(k=27)^38 h_k\n        =3A+2r_0+r=4lambda A<60.                    (E2)\n\nAll these steps exceed1. Define the nonnegative physical action\n\n    Aperp=sum_(k=27)^38 h_k||v_k||^2+||v_39||^2,\n    Mperp=max_(27<=i<=39)||v_i||^2.\n\nWeighted Cauchy--Schwarz and the actual recursion imply, for every\ni,j in I_loc,\n\n    ||v_i||<=sqrt(Aperp),\n    ||z_j-z_i||<=sqrt(Hloc Aperp),\n    Qperp_ij>=-sqrt(Hloc) Aperp.\n\nConsequently, if Aperp<=E_rec/(10000 sqrt(Hloc)), full legality\nwould imply Qbase_ij<=E_rec/10000 on every local pair, contradicting\nR1. Therefore ANY such legal extension must satisfy\n\n    Aperp > E_rec/(10000 sqrt(Hloc)) > E_rec/80000.   (E3)\n\nThe elementary bound ||z_j-z_i||<=Hloc sqrt(Mperp) also gives\n\n    Mperp > E_rec/(10000 Hloc) > E_rec/600000.        (E4)\n\nThis is a quantitative necessary change in the actual gradient state.\nIt covers arbitrary added dimension, added component values, common\noptimum, and final gradient; it is not a separability assumption.\nThe projection must be exactly the specified fixed-child affine\ngeometry. Arbitrary in-plane changes are outside E3-E4.\n\nAperp is NOT a proved additive loss of initial squared distance.\nNeither orthogonal projection nor GD forces its new component to be\nthe gradient of an independent function. A separate normalization\nargument is required before translating E3 into an all-depth payoff\nbound. The next construction gives such a cost exactly for one\nexplicit changed family, without claiming a universal distance theorem.\n\n## C1. A changed family that closes at every prescribed finite depth\n\nWe now give an actual constructive alternative. It retains the complete\ncurrent31 vectors as its initial PROJECTION, appends a scalar coordinate,\nand replaces all values by the formula below. Thus the old two queried\nvalue deficits and two lifted-block offsets are not asserted unchanged:\ntheir replacement, the residuals, optimum and true radius are explicit.\nThe projected vectors include the current31 nonzero residuals exactly.\n\nFix sigma in [9999/10000,1] and any integer depth J>=1. Set\n\n    h^(0)=h_31, N_0=31, Cword_0=lambda^3 A,\n    Cword_j=lambda^(j+3) A, N_j=32*2^j-1,\n    b_j=1+theta Cword_j,\n    h^(j+1)=(h^(j),b_j,h^(j)),   0<=j<J,\n    S_j=sum h^(j)=Cword_j-1.\n\nThe capacity formula follows by induction from\n1+sum h^(j+1)=2Cword_j+theta Cword_j=lambda Cword_j.\nIn particular h^(1) is EXACTLY the current physical h_63, since\ntheta Cword_0=lambda^2 A=r. Every entry in every h^(j) exceeds1.\n\nChoose the two parameters ONCE for the enclosing depth J:\n\n    mu=1/[2(S_J+1)], c_pad^2=32(S_J+5)/mu^3,\n    c_pad>0.\n                                                            (C1)\n\nThey remain fixed at all inner nodes and throughout each run. For\nevery level j and 0<=i<=N_j, define its scalar clock\n\n    p^(j)_0=1,\n    p^(j)_i=product_(0<=t<i)(1-mu h^(j)_t).\n                                                            (C2)\n\nEvery factor lies in (0,1), and finite induction on the factors gives\np^(j)_i>=1-mu sum_(t<i)h^(j)_t>1/2. If i!=l are two\nordinary indices, telescoping the actual scalar recursion and using\nh_t>1 proves\n\n    |p^(j)_i-p^(j)_l|>mu/2.                          (C3)\n\nFor the optimum clock p_star=0, the difference from any ordinary\nclock is greater than1/2 and therefore greater than mu/2.\n\nThe projected base vectors at level0 are the exact current data\n\n    Zbase^(0)_i=Z_i, Gbase^(0)_i=G_i, 0<=i<=31.\n\nZbase and Gbase name projected points and gradients. All these arrays\ndepend on the fixed enclosing parameters J,sigma; those are suppressed\nin the notation. Cword is a physical word capacity, not the benchmark C_n.\nAt level j let the base dimension be d_j=9*2^j, let\nP_j^base=Zbase^(j)_0, and put\n\n    zeta_j=(1-mu b_j)p^(j)_(N_j)>0,\n    E_j^base u=(u,0), F_j^base u=(0,zeta_j u),\n    t_j^base=E_j^base(Zbase^(j)_(N_j)-b_j Gbase^(j)_(N_j))\n                                             -F_j^base P_j^base.\n\nDefine the next projected arrays, for 0<=i<=N_j, by\n\n    Zbase^(j+1)_i=E_j^base Zbase^(j)_i,\n    Gbase^(j+1)_i=E_j^base Gbase^(j)_i,\n    Zbase^(j+1)_(N_j+1+i)=t_j^base+F_j^base Zbase^(j)_i,\n    Gbase^(j+1)_(N_j+1+i)=F_j^base Gbase^(j)_i.              (C4)\n\nThese are explicit Euclidean vectors, so their entire Gram matrices\nare PSD, including all singular-block range conditions. The definition\ngives the physical bridge exactly and preserves every other GD update.\nThe terminal gradient is chosen by the same formula as the right copy;\nall its incoming rows will be checked too.\n\nThe FULL changed arrays at level j, in dimension d_j+1, are\n\n    Xnew_i=(Zbase^(j)_i,c_pad p^(j)_i),\n    Gnew_i=(Gbase^(j)_i,c_pad mu p^(j)_i),\n    fnew_i=(c_pad^2 mu/2)(p^(j)_i)^2,\n    Ynew_i=(Zbase^(j)_i-Gbase^(j)_i,c_pad(1-mu)p^(j)_i),\n    delta_new,i=(c_pad^2 mu(1-mu)/2)(p^(j)_i)^2\n                                      -||Gbase^(j)_i||^2/2.\n                                                            (C5)\n\nThe associated height is also explicit:\n\n    height_new,i=fnew_i-<Gnew_i,Xnew_i>+||Gnew_i||^2/2\n       =||Gbase^(j)_i||^2/2-<Gbase^(j)_i,Zbase^(j)_i>\n                       -(c_pad^2 mu(1-mu)/2)(p^(j)_i)^2.\n\nThe optimum is (0,0,0). These formulas give the full vector, value,\nresidual and energy state; the minimizer and distance are proved below.\nThe old input values are deliberately reselected to fnew_i. There is\nno assertion that kappa31 or its positive endpoint energy survives\nthis change of child.\n\n## C2. Explicit similarity join and full-state transport\n\nIdentify the level-(j+1) ambient space as\nR^(d_j) direct-sum R^(d_j) direct-sum R. One static function, exact minimizer and actual normalizing cost\n\nAt a fixed level j define, for an arbitrary vector v in the full\nambient space,\n\n    Hnew(v)=||v||^2/2\n               +max{0,max_(0<=i<=N_j)(<Ynew_i,v>-delta_new,i)},\n    Fnew(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-Hnew(v)). If\nH_i(v)=||v||^2/2+<Ynew_i,v>-delta_new,i, then\n\n    H_l(Gnew_i)-H_i(Gnew_i)=Qnew_(i,l)<0, l!=i.\n\nThe zero affine term gives the optimum quadratic and obeys the same\ncomparison. Thus the i-th quadratic is active at Gnew_i,\nXnew_i belongs to its subdifferential, and conjugacy gives\nFnew(Xnew_i)=fnew_i and grad Fnew(Xnew_i)=Gnew_i. All sample values,\ngradients and GD updates therefore belong to ONE static objective.\n\nThe row star->i in C8 is -delta_new,i<0. Hence all delta_new,i\nare strictly positive. Relative to (18)\nand (20), the forward row changes by lift7_i-n lift7_j and the reverse row\nchanges by n lift7_i-lift7_j.\n\nFor a forward row with early source i<4 the change is nonpositive.\nFor late sources i=4,5 and early receivers j=0,1,2, the sum of the\nsource height and positive mixed term is bounded above by\n\n    chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)+eps_lift\n       <=chi k[-m^2/R^2+c_lift D] <= 0.             (37)\n\nHere b_j<=b_0=R^2-m, since b_0-b_1=am>0 and\nb_0-b_2=m(m-1)+z>0. The last bracket is negative by (11):\nm^2/R^2>(341/100)^2/(71/5)>4/5, while c_lift D<11/1000.\nFor j=3 the mixed term is zero and (36) applies. Late sources 6,7\nand early receivers have a nonpositive product. Sources 4,5 and late\nreceivers also have a nonpositive product. Their heights remain\nnonpositive by (36).\n\nThe only other forward cases are sources 6,7 and late receivers 4+j.\nEquation (19) gets the extra term (1-n)eps_lift. After division by k\nthis is at most c_lift chi D<1/800000. At atomic j=0,2, the original\nproof has a negative margin at least nE_j/25>1/400, since its product\nratio is less than (6/5)(4/5)=24/25. At j=1 the source-6 margin is\nd_atom/2>1/2400. The source-7 margin is, by (31),\nnz(1-rho Caff)>1/80000. These are all larger than the possible\nincrement. At j=3 the source-6 expression is at most\n-chi z/2-d_atom and the source-7 expression is at most -chi D/2.\nAdding c_lift chi D leaves both nonpositive, also at chi=0.\nThis covers all forward rows of the lifted child.\n\nFor reverse rows with early receivers, a late source has nonpositive\nmixed term and height bounded in (36); early sources are unchanged.\nThe zero-residual receiver 3 leaves just this nonpositive height.\nFor late receivers, early sources have nonpositive mixed term, and\nthe receiver's increased value helps. If both source and receiver\nare late, the original expression (21) changes by\n(n-1)eps_lift<=0. This covers every reverse row, including all\nendpoint directions. Consequently all 272 original ordered rows of\nthe lifted fifteen-step child, with its optimum zero, are legal.\n\n## 15. Complete boundary table for the actual next join\n\nIndex the four atoms of the fifteen-step child by b=0,1,2,3 and write\nits original index as 4b+j, 0<=j<=3. Put\n\n    s_b=(1,k,n,nk),\n    d_b=(d_atom,k d_atom,0,0).\n\nHere d_b is a value-deficit coefficient, not an outgoing vector.\nFor the atom let Patom_j=<p,g_j>, b_j=<p,y_j>, l_j=<w,y_j> as before,\nand define Zeta_j=<w,g_j>. Their entries include\n\n    Patom_j=(m,m,z,D),\n    Zeta_j=(0,0,rho eta,N),\n    l_j=(D,D,rho e,0).\n\nLet barY_i=Z_i-J_i, barH_i=fhat_i-<J_i,Z_i>+||J_i||^2/2,\nand barDelta_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. The exact intrinsic data are\n\n    barH_(4b+j)=-d_b 1_(j=2),                b=0,2,\n    barH_(4b+j)=-chi s_b Patom_j-d_b 1_(j=2),    b=1,3,\n    barDelta_(4b+j)=s_b E_j-d_b 1_(j=2).            (38)\n\nIn particular barDelta_(4b+j)>=rho s_b E_j for every b,j.\nThe increments (28) are c_lift chi s_b D for b=1,3 and zero\notherwise. Thus every barH_i+L_i is nonpositive, and every\nbarDelta_i+L_i is nonnegative.\n\nWrite\n\n    V_i=<P_2,J_i>, Pval_i=<P_2,barY_i>,\n    U_i=<J_i,d_2>, Qval_i=<d_2,barY_i>.\n\nThe following is a complete four-block table, derived from (6), (10),\nand d_2=B_top alpha T(chi p-r_2 w):\n\n| b | V_(4b+j) | Pval_(4b+j) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Patom_j | b_j |\n| 1 | -q_axis Patom_j | -q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |\n| 2 | Cpair_1 Patom_j | Cpair_1 b_j |\n| 3 | -Cpair_1 q_axis Patom_j | -Cpair_1 q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |\n\n| b | U_(4b+j) | Qval_(4b+j) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 Zeta_j/R^2 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 l_j/R^2 |\n| 1 | Cpair_2 k(chi Patom_j-r_1 Zeta_j) | Cpair_2 k(-chi Mout_1+chi b_j-r_1 l_j) |\n| 2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 Zeta_j/R^2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 l_j/R^2 |\n| 3 | nk(chi Patom_j-r_2 Zeta_j) | nk(-chi Mout_2+chi b_j-r_2 l_j) |\n                                                               (39)\n\nFor example a vector in the left seven-step part pairs with\nB_top alpha T(chi p-r_2w) by multiplying its pairing with the old\noutgoing vector d by <P,alpha T(chi p-r_2w)>/R^2=Cpair_2.\nA vector in the right seven-step part instead gets the factor n.\nThe first table follows by taking the same old cross identity with\nE_top P. These arguments derive every table entry, without a new\nPSD relaxation or a sign assumption on an unlisted coordinate.\n\nThe signs are as follows. In blocks 0,2, Pval is positive for j<3\nand zero for j=3; U is positive only for j=2,3. In blocks 1,3,\nPval is nonpositive; U is nonnegative for j=0,1 and strictly negative\nfor j=2,3. Qval is positive in blocks 0,2 for j<3 and zero for j=3;\nit is negative in blocks 1,3 for j<3, and nonpositive for j=3.\nFor the latter sign, chi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600 and r_1 l_j>1 for j<3;\nMout_j>0. At chi=0 the endpoint entries vanish, consistently with\nthe same inequalities.\n\n## 16. Every forward cross row of the thirty-one-step parent\n\nFor any 0<=i,j<=15, (29) and the universal original-row identity (4)\ngive exactly\n\n    Q_(i,16+j)=barH_i-t_scale(barDelta_j+L_j)\n                                      +U_i Pval_j/R^2.          (40)\n\nIf the last product is nonpositive there is nothing to cancel:\nboth preceding terms are nonpositive. We check every possible\npositive product, retaining the zero-energy receivers separately.\n\nFirst suppose the receiver lies in block 0 or 2 and its atomic\nindex is j<3. Its energy is at least rho s_b E_j. The ratios of its\npositive Pval to its block scale are b_j and (Cpair_1/n)b_j, respectively.\nBoth are at most b_j. For sources in blocks 0,2 at atomic indices\n2,3, the mixed term divided by t_scale s_b E_j is at most,\nrespectively,\n\n    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tfirst_1 < (1/3)(101/100)<1/2,\n    Tfirst_2 < 1/2.                                 (41)\n\nWe used b_j/E_j<=Dlen, and the endpoint source j=3 multiplies\nthe corresponding atomic-index-2 expression by rho. Since rho>99/100,\nthese products fit within the receiver energy. The other positive\nsources can only be atomic U samples in blocks 1,3. For block 1,\nthe sum of its height and the product is at most\nchi k m[-1+Cpair_2 Pval_j/R^2]<=0. For block 3 it is at most\nchi nk m[-1+Pval_j/R^2]<=0. Here Pval_j<=R^2. Receivers at atomic\nindex 3 in blocks 0,2 have Pval=0 and are already covered.\n\nNow the receiver is in block 1 or 3. A positive product requires\na source at atomic index 2 or 3 in block 1 or 3. For an atomic-index-2\nsource, put s_source=k and theta_source=Cpair_2 if its block is 1, and put\ns_source=nk and theta_source=1 if its block is 3. Let r be r_1 or r_2\naccording to that source block. Let theta_receiver=1 in receiver\nblock 1 and Cpair_1 in receiver block 3. The source height plus product,\nbefore its nonpositive paid deficit is subtracted, is exactly\n\n    s_source { chi(theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r-z)\n             +theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r b_j/R^2 }.   (42)\n\nHere Lcoef_r means Lcoef_1 or Lcoef_2. For an endpoint source this\nwhole expression is multiplied by rho. For atomic receiver j<3,\nthe non-chi part of (42), divided by t_scale s_b E_j, is at most\n\n    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tsecond_1 < 2/5       (source block 1),\n    Tsecond_2 < 1/2                   (source block 3).          (43)\n\nFor receiver block 3 these ratios are further multiplied by\nCpair_1/n<4/5. Thus the energy rho s_b E_j pays for the non-chi part.\n\nThe chi part is nonpositive except possibly for source block 3\nand receiver block 1. In that remaining case it is at most\n\n    nk chi z/1000,\n\nby (34). The added receiver energy in (40) is\n\n    t_scale eps_lift = nk chi z (v_scale c_lift rho).\n\nIt is at least the required amount since\nv_scale c_lift rho>(99/100)(1/100)(99/100)>1/1000.\nThe same bound covers the endpoint source, whose coefficient is smaller.\nAt atomic receiver j=3, b_j=E_j=0, so this chi calculation alone\nproves the row. If chi=0 both expressions vanish. This exhausts\nall 256 forward cross rows, including every incoming terminal row.\n\n## 17. Every reverse cross row and the full original system\n\nThe other orientation is exactly\n\n    Q_(16+i,j)=t_scale(barH_i+L_i)-barDelta_j\n                                      +V_i Qval_j/R^2.         (44)\n\nThe height term is nonpositive. Products of opposite signs are\ntherefore already covered. Consider first a receiver in block 0 or 2\nwith atomic index j<3. A positive product requires a source in block\n0 or 2, whose V_i is at most m. Dividing the mixed term by the\nreceiver's s_b E_j, and using l_j/E_j<=rho, gives at most\n\n    Cpair_2 m sigma r_0 Mout_1 rho/R^4\n        < Cpair_2/3 < 3/4,                    receiver block 0,\n    m sigma r_0 Mout_2 rho/R^4\n        <= m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4,   receiver block 2.\n\nThese are smaller than rho, so barDelta_j>=rho s_b E_j suffices.\nTheir atomic endpoint receivers have Qval=barDelta=0 and leave\nonly the nonpositive height term.\n\nFor receivers in blocks 1,3, a positive product requires a source\nin blocks 1,3. Let s_source=k,nk and a_source=1,Cpair_1 in these two\nsource blocks. Its height satisfies\n\n    barH_i+L_i<=-(1-c_lift)chi s_source Patom_i.\n\nLet s_receiver=k,nk and theta_receiver=Cpair_2,1 in receiver blocks\n1,3, with r=r_1,r_2 and M_r=Mout_1,Mout_2 respectively. The height term\nplus mixed term in (44) is at most\n\n    chi Patom_i[-t_scale(1-c_lift)s_source\n           +a_source q_axis s_receiver theta_receiver M_r/R^2]\n      +a_source q_axis Patom_i s_receiver theta_receiver\n                                      (-chi b_j+r l_j)/R^2.    (45)\n\nThe coefficient in the first line is nonpositive. Indeed\na_source/s_source<=1/k, since Cpair_1/n<1. After division by s_source,\nthe positive term is at most Cpair_1 Cpair_2 for receiver block 1, and\nn Cpair_2 for receiver block 3. Both are at most n Cpair_2 because Cpair_1<n.\nOn the other hand,\n\n    t_scale(1-c_lift)=n v_scale(1-c_lift)>n Cpair_2\n\nby (32). The -chi b_j term in the second line is also nonpositive.\nFor atomic receiver j<3, its remaining term divided by s_receiver E_j\nis at most\n\n    q_axis m r_2 rho/R^2\n       =m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4<rho.\n\nThe receiving energy pays for it. At atomic index j=3 both b_j and\nl_j vanish, so the first-line estimate proves the row directly.\nThis exhausts all 256 reverse cross rows, including every outgoing\nterminal row.\n\nThe left child contributes 240 ordered within-child rows by sections\n2-8. The lifted right child contributes 240 by section 14 and scaling.\nThe two cross orientations contribute 512 by sections 16-17. The 64\noptimum incidences hold by (38) and (36): all physical heights are\nnonpositive and all energies are nonnegative. These are precisely\n1056 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,31}. No original gradient,\nqueried pair, final sample, or range condition has been removed.\n\n## 18. One global function, its minimizers, and exact normalization\n\nDefine ynew_i=Xnew_i-Gnew_i and\nDeltanew_i=Fnew_i-||Gnew_i||^2/2. On the same R^9 put\n\n    Hnew(v)=||v||^2/2\n               +max(0,max_(0<=i<=31)(<ynew_i,v>-Deltanew_i)),\n    F_31(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-Hnew(v)). Its linear term is the support function\nof the hull in (47). Capacity squared\nhas not replaced B_31. Rescaling F_31(Rx)/R^2 gives true distance one\nand the same normalized payoff; multiplication by any smoothness\nconstant L_smooth>0 gives the corresponding steps h_i/L_smooth.\nThis is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.\n\n## 19. No numerical evaluation, solver, replay, installation,\nsubordinate agent, consult or fact_submit is used. The only accepted\nmathematical input is the original full certificate identity in\nfact04219554e41d9750, Section10. Every additional claim used below is\nproved here. Earlier drafts are context, not correctness premises.\n\nThe new interface is quantitative: a negative two-step prefix cut cannot\nbe supported by an arbitrarily small repaired last-cut energy. The\noriginal cross rows of M and their actual coefficient signs impose an\nexplicit gap. Keeping the earlier first-cut budget gives a closed,\npiecewise solution of the resulting two-energy optimization, including\nzero and saturated energies. This is not a sufficiency test for the\nentire primitive cone.\n\nThe original threshold seed and exact-profile target remain open. In\nparticular, this draft does NOT claim that the excluded energy strip\ncontains a point passing every previously published scalar/triangle\ntest. That additional independence question is separated from the\nfull-cone exclusion actually proved here.\n\n## 1. Original space and notation\n\nInitially assume\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v), a,b,c>0, v>=0,\n    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,\n    C=1+sum h=4+a+b+c+v,\n    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, Hpre=2+a+b, Apre=1+a+b,\n    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t,\n    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t),\n    Rcut=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2.                 (1)\n\nThe repaired-cut results assume b*t<=1, so ell>=0. No division by ell\nwill appear in the final test. When comparing with the earlier positive\nexcess regime, write d=v-1; that comparison explicitly assumes d>0.\n\nLet P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j, s=P4=C-1, e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, and let e_i be the\nfive original standard coordinate columns. Set\n\n    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=D+sym(LT).\n\nFull feasibility means Lambda_ii=0, all twenty Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j,\nL^T e=omega, and M positive semidefinite on ALL of R^5. Here\nsym(Z)=(Z+Z^T)/2. The accepted identity is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                                   -s(s+1)||g4||^2.\n                                                               (2)\n\nThis is a polynomial identity on every formal gradient and value after\nthe GD recursion x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i is substituted. A formal substitution\nneed not itself be a legal trajectory; any use of that distinction is\nexplicit below.\n\nThe flow gives e^T M e=0: Te=e+P and\n2sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2, whereas\ne^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2. PSD forces Me=0 by applying nonnegativity to\n(e+z*q)^T M(e+z*q) for both signs of z. It also gives\n\n    |x^T M y|^2 <= (x^T M x)(y^T M y).                 (3)\n\nIf one energy is zero, the mixed term is zero, by the same polynomial\nargument. No positive-definiteness assumption is needed.\n\nDefine the two ORIGINAL cut columns\n\n    jcut=e0+e1, kcut=e3+e4,\n    S=jcut^T M jcut, Tcut=kcut^T M kcut, r=jcut^T M kcut,\n    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,\n    Bmass=Hpre*B0+beta*B1.                              (4)\n\nThus S,Tcut,B0,B1>=0 and |r|<=sqrt(S*Tcut). None of the formal gradients\nare identified, even in the boundary v=0.\n\n## 2. Exact cost of the repaired last-cut geometry\n\nConsider scalar base and auxiliary data, with saturated values\nf_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 in each array:\n\n    xB=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t),        gB=(1,1,1,-t,-t),\n    xA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,-a*b*t),\n    gA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*t,-a*b*t).                        (5)\n\nBoth arrays obey the ORIGINAL four steps, because c=(1+v)*t. Their\nnormals x_i-g_i are\n\n    yB=(Hpre,beta,0,-v*t,0),\n    yA=(0,0,0,-a*b*v*t,0).\n\nFor saturated values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)*y_j. Take their orthogonal sum\nwith weights ell and mu: positions and gradients are\n(sqrt(ell)*xB_i,sqrt(mu)*xA_i) and\n(sqrt(ell)*gB_i,sqrt(mu)*gA_i), and values are ell*fB_i+mu*fA_i.\n\nHere is the complete list of nonzero finite residuals, with repeated\nindices denoting their original ordered pairs:\n\n    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre,\n    Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,\n    Q03=-a*b*u*mu*v*t,\n    Q23=-a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.                             (6)\n\nEvery other ordered finite residual is zero. In particular Q13=0:\nthe base residual -v*t*(1+t) and the auxiliary residual\na^2*b*v*t*(1-b*t) cancel after weighting. For Q03, the positive cost\nis v*t*mu*[ell+a*b*(1+a*b*t)]=a*b*u*mu*v*t. For Q23 it is\nv*t*mu*[ell+a^2*b^2*mu]=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t. These identities also\nhold when ell=0 or v=0.\n\nThe zero optimum jet can be added. Residuals directed toward it vanish.\nThe opposite residuals are the negatives of <G_i,X_i-G_i>; at targets\n0,1,3 these inner products are respectively ell*Hpre, ell*beta, and\nv*t^2*(ell+mu*a^2*b^2), all nonnegative. The other two are zero.\n\nThese are samples of one actual global convex differentiable function\nwith 1-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer. Indeed take Kphys to be the\nconvex hull of zero and the five displayed gradient vectors, and set\n\n    Fphys(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).\n\nThe residual checks say precisely that X_i-G_i is an outward normal\nat G_i. Completing the square makes the unique maximizer the Euclidean\nprojection onto Kphys. Adding the two projection normal inequalities\ngives ||G(X)-G(Y)||^2<=<G(X)-G(Y),X-Y>, hence the projection is\n1-Lipschitz. Optimality at X and X+z bounds\nFphys(X+z)-Fphys(X)-<G(X),z> between zero and ||z||^2, proving\ndifferentiability. Convexity follows from the maximum of affine\nfunctions, and Fphys>=0 with Fphys(0)=0. This construction also covers\ndegenerate Kphys at ell=0 or v=0. It is one static function.\n\nFor any saturated-value data the squared-distance telescope in (2) is\n\n    V_h=||X0||^2-||X4+s*G4||^2.\n\nHere X4=G4 and ell+mu*a^2*b^2=a^2*b*beta. Consequently the energy is\nexactly Rcut in (1). The full identity (2), with all costs (6), gives\n\n Rcut=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*gA^T M gA+ell*mu*Bmass\n          +a*b*u*mu*v*t*Lambda03\n          +a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t*Lambda23.                 (7)\n\nThe scalar column gA in this formula is the five-entry column in (5).\nEvery summand is nonnegative for a feasible array. In particular\n\n    Rcut>=0,\n    ell*[mu^2*Tcut+mu*Bmass] <= Rcut.                  (8)\n\nNotice the cross-term-containing energy that has NOT disappeared:\n\n    gA=u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut+a*b*e,\n    gA^T M gA=||u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut||_M^2.    (9)\n\nHere ||z||_M^2 means z^T M z. Formula (9) will delimit the relaxation\nsolved below; it is not set to zero in a claimed full certificate.\n\n## 3. First-cut and first-row budgets, retained simultaneously\n\nSet\n\n    eta=b/(1+w+v), Fcut=A^2-C^2*eta^2.                  (10)\n\nThe scalar function with derivative clamp(x,[-eta,1]) and minimum zero,\nstarted at A, has gradients (1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta) for this word. Its\nendpoint equals its gradient and its primitive energy is Fcut. Its\ntwenty residuals are nonpositive, so (2) and Me=0 give\n\n    Fcut>=0,  S<=Fcut/(1+eta)^2.                       (11)\n\nThere is a second, sometimes stronger budget from the initial lifted\ncut. The original diagonal is M00=1-a^2-a*sum_(j>0)Lambda0j. The\nconstant-kernel row moment is sum_(j>0)P_j*Lambda0j=2u. Therefore full\nfeasibility implies 0<a<1. The scalar bound C*eta<=A and C>A give\na*eta<1. Put\n\n    delta=u^2-2,\n    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2,\n    chi=1/nu+1/(1+eta)^2.                              (12)\n\nThe lifted-cut accounting, reproved in Appendix A, is\n\n Erow=nu*(M00+a*(Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40))\n       +(1+eta)^2*M11\n       +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (13)\n\nThe construction and identity (13) need only 0<a<1, a*eta<1 and the\ncurrent positive a,b,c with v>=0; the stronger first-row gap from the\nappendix has its original additional hypotheses. PSD and weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz applied to sqrt(M00),sqrt(M11) show\n\n    S<=(sqrt(M00)+sqrt(M11))^2<=chi*Erow.\n\nDefine the completely explicit nonnegative budget\n\n    Gcap=min(Fcut/(1+eta)^2, chi*Erow).                 (14)\n\nThus S<=Gcap. A negative Fcut or Erow already excludes feasibility.\nNo choice between the two actual functions changes the multiplier\narray: both inequalities must hold for the SAME original M and Lambda.\n\nFor d=v-1>0 retain the original quantitative result2948d4cd3660413f\nexactly: with Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,\nWrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow), and a>sqrt(2)-1,\n\n u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu),\n Erow>=nu*[u*delta/(sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)\n                                 +sqrt(Qenergy))]^2>0. (15)\n\nHere 0<=Qenergy<1 is necessary. If additionally a*b<=1 and c*d<=1,\nthe strict consequences remain Erow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and\nb<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)). Appendix A supplies their full proof,\nincluding Qenergy=0. No reversal or extension to other endpoint regimes\nis inferred from (15).\n\n## 4. The original cross rows price a prefix deficit\n\nLet\n\n    Dprefix=N^2*b^2-A^2.                               (16)\n\nThis is the negative of the primitive energy of the actual two-step\nprefix (u,beta), on the scalar cut started at A with gradients\n(1,1,-b). To apply the full identity, extend these data FORMALLY by\nconstant gradient -b through the last two updates and affine function\nvalues. The extension need not be legal; this is a substitution in (2).\nIts full primitive energy equals the prefix energy A^2-N^2*b^2, as\ndirect substitution or telescoping the two constant-gradient updates\nshows.\n\nThe formal gradient column is beta*jcut-b*e. The only negative\nresiduals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta. The only positive residuals are\n\n    Q03=Q13=w*b*beta,\n    Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta.\n\nAll remaining ordered residuals are zero. Hence (2) gives the EXACT\nfull-array identity\n\n Dprefix=b*beta*[w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)\n                         +(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14)]\n             -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).           (17)\n\nIndependently, expand the original rows0 and1 against kcut. Since\nT e0=u*e-a*e0 and\nT e1=beta*e-beta*e0-b*e1, one obtains\n\n 2*e0^T M kcut=a*B0-Lambda03-(v+1)*Lambda04,\n 2*e1^T M kcut=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(v+1)*Lambda14.\n\nThus\n\n w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14)\n    =w*(Apre*B0+b*B1-2r)\n                         -v*(w-1)*(Lambda04+Lambda14).\n\nSubstitution in (17) yields\n\n Dprefix/beta=(b*w*Apre-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*r-beta*S\n                -u*Lambda20-b*v*(w-1)*(Lambda04+Lambda14). (18)\n\nThe two subtracted terms have their original nonnegative signs. Define\n\n    kappa=max((b*w*Apre-u)/Hpre, b^2*w/beta)>0,\n    tau=kappa/mu, zeta=b*w/mu.                          (19)\n\nSince B0,B1>=0, their two positive-or-signed contributions in (18) are\nat most kappa*Bmass. This remains valid if the first coefficient is\nnegative. No outgoing terminal coefficient was discarded before its\ncross-row contribution was accounted for.\n\nIf ell>0 put Ecut=Rcut/ell. Combining (8), (18), and (3), with\nx=sqrt(S), y=mu*sqrt(Tcut), gives\n\n Dprefix<=beta*tau*Ecut-beta*tau*y^2\n                       +2*beta*zeta*x*y-beta^2*x^2,\n    0<=y^2<=Ecut, 0<=x^2<=Gcap.                       (20)\n\nThis is the new simultaneous energy/sign interface. Dropping r would\nlose its mixed term. Replacing small positive energy by a zero row would\nalso be invalid; (20) does neither.\n\n## 5. Complete exact decision of the two-energy optimization\n\nFor Earg,Garg>=0 define Psi(Earg,Garg) as follows. The fixed positive constants\nbeta,tau,zeta are those in (19).\n\nIf zeta^2<=beta*tau, set\n\n    Psi(Earg,Garg)=beta*tau*Earg.                               (21)\n\nIf zeta^2>beta*tau, set\n\n Psi(Earg,Garg)=beta*tau*Earg+beta*(zeta^2/tau-beta)*Garg\n                         when Garg<=tau^2*Earg/zeta^2;\n          2*beta*zeta*sqrt(Earg*Garg)-beta^2*Garg\n                         when tau^2*Earg/zeta^2<=Garg\n                                      <=zeta^2*Earg/beta^2;\n          zeta^2*Earg       when Garg>=zeta^2*Earg/beta^2.       (22)\n\nAt the shared endpoints the expressions agree. These formulas include\nEarg=0 or Garg=0 without division by either energy. In particular Psi(0,Garg)=0\nand Psi(Earg,0)=beta*tau*Earg.\n\nHere is a direct proof that Psi is exactly the maximum in (20). For\nfixed y, the unconstrained optimal x is zeta*y/beta, truncated at\nsqrt(Garg). Completing the square in x gives\n\n -beta^2*(x-zeta*y/beta)^2+(zeta^2-beta*tau)*y^2.\n\nIf the last coefficient is nonpositive, the maximum is zero, attained\nat x=y=0, which proves (21). Otherwise this expression increases along\nthe unconstrained x optimum until y reaches sqrt(Earg) or x reaches\nsqrt(Garg). On the latter boundary the concave quadratic in y is maximized\nat min(zeta*sqrt(Garg)/tau,sqrt(Earg)); this value is at least the entry point\nbeta*sqrt(Garg)/zeta because zeta^2>beta*tau. These alternatives give the\nthree cases in (22), respectively. They also prove attainment in the\nclosed rectangle, rather than only an upper bound.\n\nThe final division-free necessary condition is\n\n                  ell*Dprefix <= Psi(Rcut,ell*Gcap).   (23)\n\nFor ell>0 it follows from (20) because Psi is homogeneous of degree one\nin (Earg,Garg). For ell=0 the geometry still gives Rcut>=0, and (23) reads\n0<=Psi(Rcut,0)=beta*tau*Rcut. It is deliberately vacuous about the\nprefix at that boundary. There is no cancellation of a zero factor.\n\nThe maximization just solved is the stated TWO-ENERGY relaxation, not\nthe full original feasibility problem. For example (7) retains the\nadditional energy (9), as well as two priced original multipliers.\nAttaining (21) or (22) alone need not satisfy those terms, the remaining\nrows, calibration, or all twenty signs.\n\n## 6. A concrete gap strip in the remaining bc<=1 branch\n\nThere is a particularly simple specialization, with NO ordering of\nthe endpoint steps. If b*c<=1, then\n\n (b*w*Apre-u)/Hpre-b^2*w/beta\n                       =u*(b*c-1)/(beta*Hpre)<=0.\n\nConsequently kappa=b^2*w/beta. Put\n\n    gamma=w/mu=w*(1+v)/(w+v)>=1.\n\nWhen v>0, gamma>1. Formula (22) simplifies to\n\n Psi(Earg,Garg)=b^2*gamma*Earg+beta^2*(gamma-1)*Garg\n                           if beta^2*Garg<=b^2*Earg;\n          2*b*beta*gamma*sqrt(Earg*Garg)-beta^2*Garg\n                           if b^2*Earg<=beta^2*Garg<=b^2*gamma^2*Earg;\n          b^2*gamma^2*Earg    if beta^2*Garg>=b^2*gamma^2*Earg.  (24)\n\nFor v=0 all three expressions have the common maximum b^2*Earg, as\nalso supplied by (21). In all cases Psi(Earg,Garg)<=b^2*gamma^2*Earg. Thus\n\n                Rcut>=ell*Dprefix/(b^2*gamma^2)         (25)\n\nwhenever b*c<=1. This is substantive when ell>0 and Dprefix>0. It\nexcludes the entire full-cone region\n\n C>=193/25, a,b,c>0, v>=0, b*t<1, b*c<=1, Dprefix>0,\n             0<=Rcut<ell*Dprefix/(b^2*gamma^2).         (26)\n\nThe capacity is variable; no upper-capacity slice or endpoint square\nis used. Every full-support array is excluded there, not just a center,\nsparse ansatz, chosen rank or supplied covariance fiber.\n\nA simpler threshold strip can be stated without Dprefix. If b>=1,\nb*c<=1, v>0 and ell>0, then b*gamma=b*w/mu<beta. Also N=A+beta and\n\n Dprefix/beta^2\n    =b^2+2*b^2*A/beta+(b^2-1)*A^2/beta^2\n    >=1+A=3+a,\n\nbecause beta<=2b. Here Dprefix>0. Therefore every feasible array in\nthis regime obeys the STRICT bound\n\n                         Rcut>ell*(3+a).              (27)\n\nIn particular the whole strip 0<=Rcut<=ell*(3+a) is excluded at every\nC>=193/25, with v>0 free and no assumption that either endpoint lies\nin a previously excluded square or prism. This is a resolved energy\nstrip of the original cone. It does not assert that this strip survives\nevery older scalar witness family. No excluded square/prism is a premise.\n\nAt Rcut=0 and ell>0 the general result (23), even without b*c<=1,\nforces N*b<=A. Thus saturation of the repaired last-cut energy has an\nexplicit prefix-cut consequence, with its quantitative neighborhood\ncontrolled by (21)--(23). This does not assert arbitrary prefix or tail\nheredity away from that energy budget.\n\n## 7. Relation to full calibration and the uncovered freedom\n\nFor d=v-1>0, let p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T. Then Tp=e, and\ntelescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 proves\n\n                  p^T M p=Qenergy.                    (28)\n\nThe columns e,e0,jcut,kcut,p form a basis: any linear combination of\nthe first four has equal entries at indices3 and4, whereas p3-p4 is\n-a*b*c*v, which is nonzero. Thus full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the\nFOUR-by-FOUR Gram matrix of e0,jcut,kcut,p. All mixed entries matter.\n\nIn particular (7) retains the full quadratic expression (9), not just\nS and Tcut. The entry r is retained through (18)--(20), but those\ninequalities do not determine e0^T M kcut or kcut^T M p. The earlier\nrow0 identity prices e0^T M p by actual incoming coefficients; it does\nnot determine these other entries. A relaxed optimizer from (22) can\nfail these remaining joint requirements.\n\nHere is a complete coordinate check showing that no skew/sign freedom\nhas been silently removed. For positive steps choose P3<rho<s and put\n\n phi_i=1+P_i, weight_i=2h_i/(rho-P_i) for i<4,\n weight_4=2s/(s-rho), W=sum weight_i,\n Lambda^c_ij=phi_i*weight_i*weight_j/W for i!=j.\n\nThe sums sum weight_i*(P_i-rho)=0 and\nsum phi_i*weight_i=(1+rho)*W show that this strictly positive center\nhas the required flow and constant-kernel row moments. They do NOT\nshow PSD. Let E_diff have columns e_i-e4, i0 through3, and F_null\nhave columns e_j-(1-P_j/s)e0-(P_j/s)e4, j1 through3. All real\nflow/kernel completions are exactly\n\n    L=L^c+E_diff*X*F_null^T, X any real4by3 matrix.     (29)\n\nIndeed the difference annihilates e and P on the right and e on the\nleft, and these two displayed bases describe precisely those spaces.\nThe twenty signs remain the simultaneous inequalities\n\n    (E_diff*X*F_null^T)_ij<=Lambda^c_ij, every i!=j.    (30)\n\nLet B_pick=(e0,e1,e2,e3), Rmap=F_null^T*T*B_pick and\nK_c=B_pick^T M^c B_pick. Then\n\n K=K_c+sym(X*Rmap),\n M=[[K,-K*1],[-1^T*K,1^T*K*1]],\n q=(p0-p4,p1-p4,p2-p4,p3-p4)^T,\n q^T K q=Qenergy.                                     (31)\n\nFull M>=0 is equivalent to K>=0. Since T^(-1)P=e-p,\nker(F_null^T T)=span(e,p), so Rmap has rank3 and kernel span(q), with\nq nonzero. For any proposed calibrated K put H=K-K_c and\n\n Szero=[q*(Hq)^T-(Hq)*q^T]/(q^T q).\n\nThen Szero*q=-Hq, and every real completion is\n\n X=(H+Szero+Z)*Rmap^T*(Rmap*Rmap^T)^(-1),\n                 Z^T=-Z, Z*q=0.                      (32)\n\nMultiplication by Rmap verifies this identity; conversely X*Rmap-H\nis skew and has value -Hq on q. The free skew space on q-perp is\nthree-dimensional. None of its three parameters, none of the twenty\nsigns, and no original gradient coordinate has been deleted. At\nQenergy=0, K>=0 forces Kq=0 without division by that energy.\n\nThe new gap excludes every choice in (29)--(32) over the region (26).\nOutside it, a calibrated PSD K, a positive center, and an optimizer of\nthe cut relaxation are still three separate requirements. No full\ntwenty-sign/high-capacity completion has been produced or excluded.\nAt zero physical steps one uses the original full matrix and (7)--(23);\nthe positive-step coordinate paragraph and its independent p column\nare not invoked. In particular v=0 never identifies g3 with g4 in a\nformal certificate.\n\n## 8. Direct-proof and counterexample screens\n\nThe exact endpoint-saturation route was recalled and not re-proved as\na new result; shared6fbfca5386425d02 already handles its conditional\nthree-step interface. The present mechanism instead retains a positive\ncut energy and its actual cross rows.\n\nA first attempt, on positive a,b,c,d with a<sqrt(2)-1 and b*c<=1,\nuses row0 and the quadratic energy after dropping a nonnegative incoming\nterm. Writing m=M00 and Dsmall=2-u^2>0, it gives\nu*(m+Dsmall)<=2*sqrt(m*Qenergy). This particular bound minimizes at\nm=Dsmall and yields Qenergy>=u^2*(2-u^2), equivalently\nC*b*c*d<=a+2. It is the known scalar prefix-clipping condition, and is\nnot counted as a new obstruction. No redundancy of the entire row0\nsystem is inferred from this one optimized relaxation.\nAn attempted rational boundary example for independence of the new\ngap failed the pre-existing partial-return inequality C*b*c*d<=2+a;\nit is not a survivor or a seed. No numerical/model run was made.\n\nThe identities were checked against ell=0, Rcut=0, S=0, Tcut=0,\nQenergy=0, b*c=1 and v=0. The general kappa in (19) retains the\nb*c>1 case. The smaller kappa and gamma formulas are used only when\nb*c<=1. All actual residual signs were enumerated in (6); the prefix\nextension in Section4 is explicitly formal, and its positive residuals\nare retained rather than asserted legal. The solved optimization is\nlabeled a relaxation throughout.\n\nTargeted fact_search found no accepted result for this interface.\nMatlas searches returned unrelated matrix/optimization statements;\nweb fallback recalled the already audited composition literature. No\nexternal theorem is imported and no new literature-based branch is\nrejected. The proof uses only the accepted primitive identity plus\nthe complete elementary derivations here.\n\nThis round gives the exact cross-row/cut optimization (21)--(23), the\nfull-cone gap strip (26)--(27), and the explicit remaining three-vector\nenergy and full calibration/sign interface (9),(28)--(32). It does not\ngive an actual threshold seed, a full cover of the remaining scalar\ndomain, general reversal, or a verified original-target result.\n\n## Appendix A. Reproduced first-row proof\n\nThe following proof text is reproduced to retain the original hypotheses\nand quantitative bounds of2948d4cd3660413f. It is part of this joint\nunverified draft, not a citation to an accepted predecessor. The current\nmain text separately notes where its accounting construction extends\nto v>=0; the strict positive-excess conclusions remain exactly scoped.\n\n### 1. Statement and notation\n\nWrite a positive-excess four-step word as\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),    a,b,c,d>0,\n    C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d.\n\nSuppose it admits the full primitive certificate of accepted fact\n04219554e41d9750. Suppose additionally that a>sqrt(2)-1. Define\n\n    u=1+a,             delta=u^2-2,\n    H=3+c+d,           eta=b/H,\n    R=a+3+1/a,         k=u*delta/a,\n    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=R-k*eta-C^2*eta^2,\n    Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,\n    Wrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d),\n    Krow=max(u,a*Wrow).\n\nFull feasibility forces a<1, a*eta<1, nu>0, and\n0<=Qenergy<1. The following quantitative necessary condition holds:\n\n    u*delta <= Krow*Erow/nu + 2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).          (A)\n\nIn particular,\n\n    Erow >= nu * [u*delta /\n          (sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)+sqrt(Qenergy))]^2 > 0.  (B)\n\nThus equality in the previously used lifted middle-cut condition Erow>=0\nis impossible throughout this initial-step regime. A version avoiding\nsquare roots is\n\n    Erow>=0,\n    [max(u*delta*nu-Krow*Erow,0)]^2 <= 4*Qenergy*Erow*nu.     (C)\n\nIf a*b<=1 and c*d<=1, the simpler strict necessary inequality is\n\n    Erow > nu*delta^2/u^2,                                 (D)\n\nequivalently\n\n    b < (a+3)*(C-a-2) / ((a+1)*(C+a+2)).                (E)\n\nThe last two product assumptions are explicit hypotheses of this\nsimplification. They are not silently declared lossless for the\noriginal problem. Conditions(A)--(C) do not require them. The separate\nassumption a,b,c,d>0 is also retained explicitly; no assertion about a\nzero step or a step at most one is being imported from an unverified\ncompactness draft.\n\n### 2. Original formal certificate and its constant kernel\n\nPut P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_4=C-1, and let e be the five-entry all-ones\ncolumn. Write e_i for the i-th original standard coordinate of R^5,\nwith indices0 through4. Let T have diagonal1, entry h_t at(i,t) for t<i, and zero\nentries above the diagonal. The original twenty multipliers satisfy\n\n    Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,\n    L=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L^T e=omega,\n    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0 on ALL of R^5.               (1)\n\nThe identity in accepted04219554e41d9750 is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                      =sum_(i,j) M_ij <g_i,g_j>,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)\n          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i.                                (2)\n\nSince Te=e+P and sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2,\n\n    e^TDe=-s^2-sum h_i^2,\n    omega^TTe=s^2+sum h_i^2,\n    e^TMe=0.\n\nFor every real v, the nonnegative quadratic(e+t*v)^T M(e+t*v)\nhas zero constant coefficient. Considering both signs of t forces\nv^TMe=0, hence Me=0. The initial row of this identity is\n\n    sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j=2h0.                        (3)\n\nTo check(3) without any suppressed flow condition, direct expansion\ngives(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 for i<4. At i=0,\nP0=0 and(LP)_0=-sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j.\n\nLet Aout=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j and m=M00. Its original diagonal is\n\n    m=1-a^2-a*Aout.                                    (4)\n\nIndeed the first column of T is(1,u,u,u,u)^T, so(LT)_00\n=(1-u)Aout=-a*Aout. Since m,Aout>=0 and a>0, (4) gives\na<=1. Equality would give m=Aout=0, contradicting(3). Thus a<1.\n\nFor any positive semidefinite real matrix, the two-vector inequality\n\n    |v^T M w|^2 <= (v^T M v)*(w^T M w)                 (5)\n\nfollows by applying nonnegativity to(v+t*w)^T M(v+t*w).\nIf w^T M w=0, the linear coefficient must vanish; otherwise minimizing\nin t proves(5). This argument does not assume positive definiteness.\n\n### 3. Two exact scalar energies\n\nHere clamp(x,[-eta,1]) means min(1,max(-eta,x)).\nThe scalar function with this derivative and minimum0\nhas the actual trajectory\n\n    x=(2+a,1,-b,-(2+d)*eta,-eta),\n    g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).\n\nHere b=(3+c+d)*eta. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and\n1-Lipschitz. At every displayed sample its value is\nf_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. The first step gives x1=1; the other three GD\nupdates follow directly from the displayed relation for b.\n\nFor any GD data with saturated values\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, telescoping the squared distances gives\n\n    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2.                        (6)\n\nIn detail, the first two sums in(2) reduce to\n2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2-2s*f4;\nthe first two terms telescope to||x0||^2-||x4||^2.\nSubstitution of f4 then gives(6). The scalar trajectory has x4=g4,\nso its energy is(2+a)^2-C^2*eta^2. By(1)--(2) it is nonnegative.\nConsequently\n\n    C*eta<=2+a,\n    a*eta<=a*(2+a)/C<3/5<1,                           (7)\n\nbecause 0<a<1 and C>5. This proves nu>0.\n\nNext define the actual unit-quadratic gradient column\n\n    p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T.\n\nIt satisfies Tp=e. Since Le=0,\n\n    p^T M p=p^T D p=1-C^2*p4^2=Qenergy.               (8)\n\nFor the telescoping equality, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i gives\nh_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2; the terminal diagonal\nthen supplies-s(s+2)p4^2. Full PSD gives Qenergy>=0.\nAll a,b,c,d are positive, so Qenergy<1.\n\n### 4. Complete lifted-cut data and its exact multiplier cost\n\nLet U=(1,0), V=(0,1) be orthonormal physical vectors. Set\n\n    z=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V.\n\nThen\n\n    ||z||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a,\n    <U,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*eta.\n\nUse the five two-dimensional data\n\n    X=(U+u*z, U, -b*U, -(2+d)*eta*U, -eta*U),\n    G=(z, U, -eta*U, -eta*U, -eta*U),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (9)\n\nEvery GD update has the original step length. For these saturated\nvalues the original ordered interpolation residual is exactly\n\n    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.\n\nHere is a check of all twenty ordered finite pairs, grouped by their\ntarget j. Targets1 and4 have zero normal. At target0 the normal is\nU+a*z. The incoming pair(1,0) has residual0, whereas each incoming\npair(i,0), i=2,3,4, has residual-a*nu. At target2 the normal is\n-(b-eta)U. The only nonzero finite residual there is\nQ_12=-(1+eta)(b-eta). At target3 the normal is\n-(1+d)*eta*U. The only nonzero finite residual there is\nQ_13=-(1+eta)(1+d)*eta. Thus all twenty residuals are nonpositive.\nThe zero optimum jet can also be added: Q_(i,star)=0 by the chosen\nvalues; the opposite inequalities follow from the same normals and\na*eta<1. These optimum pairs are not additional multipliers in(1).\n\nFor completeness these are actual jets of one global smooth convex\nfunction. Let Kphys=conv{0,z,U,-eta*U} and define\n\n    F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).\n\nThe comparisons just made say that X_i-G_i is an outward normal\nto Kphys at G_i. Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer\nas the Euclidean projection of X onto Kphys. If projections at X,Y\nare G,H, adding their normal inequalities gives\n||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>, and hence ||G-H||<=||X-Y||.\nTo see differentiability directly, optimality at X and X+v bounds\nF(X+v)-F(X)-<G,v> between0 and<G(X+v)-G,v>, which is at most||v||^2.\nThus the gradient is the projection and is1-Lipschitz. The function\nis convex as a maximum of affine functions of X; it has minimum0 at0\nbecause0 belongs to Kphys. It realizes all of(9).\n\nBy(6), its primitive energy is\n\n    ||U+u*z||^2-C^2*eta^2\n      =a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2=Erow.            (10)\n\nLet\n\n    Bin=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40.\n\nIn the U coordinate the gradient column is-eta*e+(1+eta)*e1;\nin the V coordinate it is sqrt(nu)*e0. The constant kernel proved\nin Section2 therefore gives the full matrix remainder\n(1+eta)^2*M11+nu*m. Substituting every nonzero residual above into\nthe original identity(2) yields the exact accounting identity\n\n    Erow=nu*(m+a*Bin)+(1+eta)^2*M11\n       +(1+eta)*eta*((2+c+d)*Lambda12+(1+d)*Lambda13). (11)\n\nEvery term on the right is nonnegative. In particular\n\n    0<=m<=m+a*Bin<=Erow/nu.                              (12)\n\nNo incoming coefficient has been set to zero. Identity(11) prices the\noriginal incoming coefficients explicitly.\n\n### 5. The original first row couples the two energies\n\nDefine the weighted incoming combination\n\n    Bweight=(1+b)*Lambda20+(1-b*c)*Lambda30\n                                    +(1+b*c*d)*Lambda40.\n\nThe exact first-row identity is\n\n    2*e0^T M p = u*(m-delta)+a^2*Bweight.             (13)\n\nHere e0 denotes the original first coordinate column of R5. A direct\nderivation avoids any coordinate reduction. Since Tp=e and Le=0,\nMp=Dp+T^T L^T p/2. Also Te0=u*e-a*e0, giving\n\n    2*e0^T M p=2(1-a^2)-a*p^T L e0.\n\nThe flow at node0 gives Lambda10=Aout+2u-Bin. Hence\n\n    p^T L e0\n      =Aout+a*Lambda10-a*b*Lambda20\n                        +a*b*c*Lambda30-a*b*c*d*Lambda40\n      =u*Aout+2a*u-a*Bweight.\n\nFinally substitute a*Aout=1-a^2-m from(4). The constant term becomes\n\n    (2-u)(1-a^2)-2a^2*u\n      =u*((1-a)^2-2a^2)=-u*delta,\n\nwhich proves(13), with all row and flow contributions retained.\n\nAll three incoming multipliers are nonnegative. Since b,c,d>0,\neach coefficient in Bweight is at most Wrow. This remains true even\nif 1-b*c is negative. Thus Bweight<=Wrow*Bin. Applying(5) with e0,p,\nand using(8), gives from(13)\n\n    u*delta <= u*m+a^2*Wrow*Bin+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)\n             <= Krow*(m+a*Bin)+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)\n             <= Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).        (14)\n\nThis is(A). Put t=sqrt(Erow/nu). Because Krow,u,delta are positive,\n(14) says\n\n    Krow*t^2+2*sqrt(Qenergy)*t-u*delta>=0.\n\nSolving this increasing quadratic for t>=0 and rationalizing its\npositive root gives(B). Its numerator is strictly positive, so Erow>0.\nMultiplying(A) by nu and squaring only when its left side after\nsubtraction is positive gives exactly(C). There is no sign-losing\nsquaring in this formulation.\n\n### 6. A rational strengthened middle-cut boundary\n\nIf c*d<=1, then Wrow=1+b. If also a*b<=1, then\na*Wrow=a+a*b<=1+a=u, so Krow=u. Since Qenergy<1 and Erow>0,\n(14) implies the strict inequality\n\n    u*delta < u*t^2+2t,       t=sqrt(Erow/nu)>0.\n\nThe right side is strictly increasing for t>=0. Its value at\nt=delta/u is\n\n    delta^2/u+2delta/u=delta*(delta+2)/u=u*delta,\n\nbecause delta+2=u^2. It follows that t>delta/u, which is(D).\nThe exact algebraic identity\n\n    Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2\n      =[(a+3-delta*eta)^2-u^2*C^2*eta^2]/u^2          (15)\n\ncan be checked by expanding the constant, linear, and quadratic\ncoefficients in eta. Those coefficients in the numerator are\n(a+3)^2, -2(a+3)*delta, and delta^2-u^2*C^2.\n\nThe first factor a+3-delta*eta is positive: by a*eta<1,\n\n    a+3-delta*eta > a+3-delta/a = 1+1/a >0.\n\nThus(D) and(15) give\n\n    a+3 > (u*C+delta)*eta.\n\nSubstitute eta=b/(C-2-a-b), whose denominator is H>0. Since\ndelta+a+3=(a+1)(a+2), the result is exactly(E). Every denominator\nin(E) is positive.\n\nIn particular this is a necessary bound on an entire step region,\nnot a condition on a sparse coefficient family. The exact stronger\ncondition(A) still retains the actual Qenergy rather than discarding\nit in favor of the upper bound1.\n\n### 7. Adversarial scope checks\n\nThe initial-step hypothesis is substantive. At a=sqrt(2)-1 the\nquantity delta is zero, and(A) supplies no positive gap. This agrees\nwith the possibility of saturated basic certificates at that endpoint.\nWe do not extend the strict conclusion by continuity across delta=0.\n\nIf b*c>1, the coefficient of Lambda30 in(13) is negative. The proof\ndoes not replace this coefficient by its absolute value or infer a\nfalse lower bound. It uses only the valid upper bound Bweight<=Wrow*Bin.\nThus(A)--(C) still apply in that regime. The simplified statement(E)\nrequires only its explicitly stated a*b and c*d hypotheses.\n\nIf Qenergy=0, formula(B) reduces to Erow>=nu*u*delta/Krow;\nno division by Qenergy occurs. If a multiplier vanishes or M has\nadditional kernels, the semidefinite form of(5) still applies. No\nstrict complementarity, full rank, or strictly positive support was used.\n\nHere is a purely symbolic test family showing why the separate tests\nErow>=0 and Qenergy>=0 cannot justify the omitted coupling. It is an\nanalytical counterexample to that shortcut, not a numerical seed proposal\nor a claim to survive the other existing necessary inequalities. Take\n\n    a=1/2, eta=7/24, C=39*sqrt(2)/7,\n    H=24*(C-5/2)/31, b=7*(C-5/2)/31, Ktail=H-3,\n    0<d<min(Ktail/2,1/Ktail,1/(C*a*b*Ktail)), c=Ktail-d.\n\nThe elementary bounds 7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 give\n193/25<39/5<C<8. Thus Ktail>0, all four excesses are positive,\na*b<1, c*d<1, and 0<C*a*b*c*d<1. Also C=5+a+b+c+d\nand eta=b/(3+c+d). Direct exact substitution gives\n\n    R-k*eta=169/32=C^2*eta^2,\n    Erow=0, Qenergy>0,\n    nu=1271/576, delta=1/4, u=3/2.\n\nYet(D) requires Erow>1271/20736. Hence none of these words has the\nfull certificate. This illustrates the active original-row obstruction;\nit is not asserted to be a newly uncovered region beyond every prior\nobstruction.
