{"id": "ab23a6e703982fef", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T11:24:39.894821+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C94 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT: ALL queried and terminal ridge regions may change. For static centered Huber-ridge sums plus a PSD quadratic on ORIGINAL H_m, m>=128, a background floor mu>=[1-1/(beta*c)]/(1+a) forces V<=beta^-2*c^-2m and original B_n*V->0, uniformly in dimension, ridge count, horizon-dependent parameters and noncommuting coupling. For mu>=2/(1+a)-1 the exact class supremum is beta^(2^(m+1)-2)*c^-2m, attained by the unit quadratic. An explicit sharper necessary floor bound prices even attaining the SAME-word best accepted Huber/single-cut value K. A concrete static 2-D run has actual outer+,-,+ then core visits, sign changes at EVERY step, noncollinear gradients, nonconstant correlations, and true radius1; its payoff loses to K. Weak-background changing gates remain OPEN.", "evidence": "# C94: actual changing gates and the price of a curved background\n\nStatus: **COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT**. No verifier call is authorized.\nThis is runtime round 2 of the current allocation, with completed count 24\nbefore this round and ceiling 25. No numerical/model evaluation, solver,\nreplay, installation or subagent was used. The original target remains open.\n\nThe only accepted mathematical predecessors used below are\n`1252b8823bb5a290` (the original interpolation convention and its necessity)\nand `b98bb807d514e80d` (the scalar lower bounds and original benchmark bound).\nEvery new assertion is derived in this one joint draft. C93 is motivation,\nnot a mathematical predecessor. The finite C59 data are not replayed or\nused as an asymptotic premise.\n\n## 1. The resolved class and conclusions\n\nAll spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. For positive\nradii r_minus and r_plus, a centered asymmetric Huber function phi has\nphi(0)=0 and derivative\n\n    phi'(s)=max(-r_minus,min(s,r_plus)).\n\nConsider one STATIC objective\n\n    F(x)=x^T A x/2 + sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j phi_j(u_j^T x),       (1)\n\nwhere A is symmetric positive semidefinite, each u_j is a unit vector,\nalpha_j>0, and\n\n    A+sum_j alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                           (2)\n\nThe number r may be zero. Dimensions, directions, both radii and weights\nmay depend arbitrarily on the horizon. Let\n\n    N0=ker A intersect intersection_j u_j^perp, E=N0^perp.\n\nThe additional hypothesis tested here is a background-curvature floor:\n\n    z^T A z >= mu ||z||^2 for all z in E, 0<=mu<=1.          (3)\n\nThere is NO restriction on which ridge regions the actual queried or\nterminal points visit. Hessians in different regions need not commute.\nDefine the actual GD iterates for a nonnegative physical word h by\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i),\n    V=2[F(x_n)-min F]/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2.                   (4)\n\nInitial points in argmin F are omitted. All terminal gradients and values\nare those of the SAME function (1).\n\nFor the ORIGINAL diagnostic words fix m>=128 and set\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), beta=1-1/m,\n    a=beta/c, ell=1+a, d_j=1+a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, N=2^m, n=N-1,\n    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, K=1/(2M_m-1).                  (5)\n\nEvery child word in this proof keeps these SAME enclosing-m parameters.\nThe main conclusions are:\n\n1. If mu>=mu_exact:=2/ell-1, the exact supremum of V over the whole\n   class (1)--(3), with all changing regions allowed, is\n\n       beta^(2N-2) c^(-2m).                                (6)\n\n   The unit quadratic attains it. The result is exact over this specified\n   class, not over unrestricted convex smooth objectives.\n\n2. Put\n\n       mu_floor=[1-1/(beta c)]/ell.                         (7)\n\n   If mu>=mu_floor, every such run satisfies\n\n       V <= beta^(-2)c^(-2m),\n       V/K < 2/[beta^2 c^m],\n       B_n V -> 0 uniformly over the class as m -> infinity. (8)\n\n   Here B_n is the ORIGINAL Zhang--Jiang denominator of accepted\n   `b98bb807d514e80d`. Both mu_floor and mu_exact tend to sqrt(2)-1,\n   with mu_floor<mu_exact for every stated m. The thresholds, dimension,\n   number of ridges and all other data may vary with m.\n\n3. There is an explicit stronger necessary condition for attaining even\n   the accepted same-word scalar value K. Define\n\n       L_m=log[beta^2 c^(2m)/(2M_m-1)],\n       mu_comp=[1-exp(L_m/N)/(beta c)]/ell.                 (9)\n\n   Every run in (1)--(3) with V>=K must have mu<=mu_comp. Moreover\n\n       1/(2m-1)<mu_comp<mu_floor,\n       mu_floor-mu_comp=[exp(L_m/N)-1]/(beta c ell)>0.      (10)\n\n4. The resolved class really contains nonlinear, noncommuting,\n   repeatedly changing queried gates on ORIGINAL H_m. Section 6 gives\n   one static two-dimensional function whose ridge visits are positive\n   outer, negative outer, positive outer, then core at every remaining\n   actual sample. Its actual gradients change sign at EVERY physical\n   step, are not collinear, and have nonconstant correlations. Its true\n   initial distance is exactly 1. Its payoff is nevertheless bounded by\n   (6), hence is strictly weaker than K on this same word.\n\nThis supplies a precise obstruction for the newly specified curved-\nbackground subclass. It does not exclude weak or singular backgrounds,\nor arbitrary nonlinear changing-region functions. The upper envelopes\nbelow are not claimed attainable outside the regime of (6).\n\n## 2. Static legality, true minimizer distance and all original pairs\n\nEvery summand in (1) is nonnegative. A Huber term vanishes precisely\nwhen its scalar argument is zero. Thus argmin F=N0 and min F=0.\nLet z0 be the orthogonal projection of x0 onto N0. Translation of every\niterate by -z0 changes neither the values nor the gradients, because\nA z0=0 and u_j^T z0=0 for all j. All gradients lie in E. We may work\non E with x0 in E; its squared norm is the TRUE squared distance in (4).\n\nFor any two points x,z define the scalar secant coefficient theta_j by\n\n    phi_j'(u_j^T x)-phi_j'(u_j^T z)\n                 =theta_j u_j^T(x-z), 0<=theta_j<=1.       (11)\n\nIf the arguments coincide take theta_j=0. The monotonicity and the\n1-Lipschitz property of clipping prove both bounds, across any number\nof region boundaries. Consequently\n\n    grad F(x)-grad F(z)=C(x,z)(x-z),\n    C(x,z)=A+sum_j alpha_j theta_j u_j u_j^T,\n    mu I_E <= C(x,z)|E <= I_E.                             (12)\n\nThis proves global convexity and 1-smoothness. It also gives the actual\nsecant matrix used below; no matrix is independently chosen along the\ntrajectory. For h>=0, diagonalizing this ONE symmetric matrix yields\n\n    ||x-z-h[grad F(x)-grad F(z)]||\n       <= q_mu(h)||x-z||,\n    q_mu(h)=max{|1-h mu|,|1-h|},                           (13)\n\nfor x,z in E. Taking z=0 and multiplying the true one-step bounds gives\n\n    ||x_n|| <= ||x0|| product_(i<n) q_mu(h_i).\n\nSmoothness at the minimizer gives 2F(x_n)<=||x_n||^2, so\n\n    V <= product_(i<n) q_mu(h_i)^2.                        (14)\n\nNo commutativity of DIFFERENT secant matrices was used in (13)--(14).\n\nFor completeness every original ordered interpolation pair is retained.\nPut f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i) and include x_star=g_star=f_star=0.\nFor a fixed i, J(z)=F(z)-<g_i,z> has its global minimum at x_i.\nThe 1-smooth descent inequality at x_j gives\n\n    J(x_i)<=J(x_j-grad J(x_j))\n            <=J(x_j)-||g_j-g_i||^2/2.\n\nRearrangement is the original residual\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.         (15)\n\nThis is the necessity argument of accepted `1252b8823bb5a290`, written\nout here to cover both optimum directions, every remote queried pair,\nboth terminal directions, and all repeated/zero-step samples.\n\nMore generally, for ANY fixed nonnegative word all of whose steps obey\nh_i>=2/(1+mu), (13) has q_mu(h_i)=h_i-1. Indeed this inequality implies\nh_i>=1, 1-h_i mu<=h_i-1, and 1-h_i mu>=-(h_i-1).\nThe bound (14) is then attained by F(x)=x^2/2, x0=1, r=0.\nThus the exact class supremum on such a word is product_i(h_i-1)^2.\nThis general finite-word observation includes the empty word. It makes\nno matching assertion for schedules that fail the displayed step condition.\n\n## 3. Exact physical word algebra and the same-word scalar comparator\n\nThe bounds needed below follow from m>=128 and elementary squaring of\n7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70:\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 2/5<a<29/70<1/2,\n    7/5<ell<99/70, 127/128<=beta<1,\n    d_1=1+beta=2-1/m>19/10,\n    d_j>2 for j>=2.                                      (16)\n\nFor the lower bound on a, a=(m-1)/[(m+1)sigma] is at least\n8890/21801>2/5. For the upper bound on c,\nc<(169/70)(129/128)<5/2. Also a<1/sigma=sigma-2<c-2.\n\nCounting a level j in H_m gives exactly 2^(m-j-1) occurrences.\nSumming the physical masses or solving M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j gives\n\n    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2), 0<A_mass<1.                           (17)\n\nIn particular 1<=M_j<=c^j and a c^j<=(c-2)M_j.\n\nWe now rederive the best Huber/single-cut comparison directly from\naccepted `b98bb807d514e80d`, without using C93 as a lemma. For a cut of\nlevel j, the numerator is (a c^j)^2. If P is its actual preceding mass,\nits denominator is\n\n    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_m-d_j-P)].                             (18)\n\nThe logarithm of (18) has second derivative\n-2/(1+P)^2-4/[1+2(S_m-d_j-P)]^2<0. Hence its minimum over the mass\ninterval from the first to the last occurrence is at one of those\nendpoints. Write M=M_(m-1). At the first occurrence P=S_j and the\nfollowing mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut value is at most\n(c-2)^2/(2M-1). At the last occurrence the preceding mass is at least\nS_(m-1) and the following mass is S_j. Its value is at most\n\n    (c-2)^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)] <= (c-2)^2/(2M-1),       (19)\n\nbecause x^2/(2x-1) is nondecreasing for x>=1 and M_j<=M.\nFinally M_m<=cM, c<5/2 and c-2<1/2 imply\n\n    (c-2)^2(2M_m-1)<=(5M-1)/4<=2M-1.\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent to M>=1. Thus every cut is no larger\nthan K=1/(2M_m-1). The global Huber term attains K, proving\n\n    K_n(H_m)=K.                                          (20)\n\nAn explicit attaining scalar function is the symmetric Huber function\nwith radius K, started at 1. Its iterates are\nx_i=1-K sum_(t<i)h_t; they all remain on\nthe positive outer ray. Its terminal normalized value is K.\n\n## 4. All gates allowed: exact supremum and the wider curvature screen\n\nThe product of the upper-endpoint factors over H_j is\n\n    U_j:=product_(h_i in H_j)(h_i-1)\n        =a^(2^j-1)c^(2^j-j-1)\n        =beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).                            (21)\n\nFor j=0 the expression gives 1. One can also check (21) by the exact\nrecursion U_(j+1)=U_j^2 a c^j.\n\nFor h>1 and 0<=mu<=1, (13) can be written\n\n    q_mu(h)=max{1-mu h,h-1}.                            (22)\n\nFor all bridge levels j>=2 the second term wins, by d_j>2. At level 1\nit wins whenever\n\n    mu>=mu_1:=2/d_1-1=1/(2m-1).                         (23)\n\nDefine q0=max{1-mu ell,a} and q1=max{1-mu d_1,beta}. The ENTIRE\nproduct bound, with no lost low-level factor, is\n\n    V <= U_m^2 (q0/a)^N (q1/beta)^(N/2).                 (24)\n\nThis holds for all 0<=mu<=1. If mu>=mu_1 it reduces exactly to\n\n    V <= beta^(-2)c^(-2m) [beta c q0]^N.                 (25)\n\nIf mu>=mu_exact=(1-a)/(1+a)=2/ell-1, q0=a. All steps satisfy the\ncondition following (15), and (25) equals beta^(2N-2)c^(-2m).\nThe unit quadratic attains it with true distance 1, proving (6).\nIt can be embedded with arbitrary flat directions without changing the\ntrue-distance quotient, because Section 2 projects onto E.\n\nFor the weaker condition mu>=mu_floor of (7), note first that\nbeta c>2 and ell<3/2. Therefore mu_floor>1/3>mu_1. Also\n1/(beta c)>a, since a beta c=beta^2<1. It follows that\nmu_floor<mu_exact and q0<=1/(beta c). Equation (25) proves the first\nbound in (8). By (17) and (20),\n\n    V/K <=(2M_m-1) beta^(-2)c^(-2m)\n           <2/[beta^2 c^m].                            (26)\n\nThis ratio is less than 1 for the stated m. The accepted original-\nbenchmark estimate in `b98bb807d514e80d` is\nB_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Since n=2^m-1<2^m,\n\n    B_n V <= beta^(-2)[c^(-2m)+4(3/c^2)^m] -> 0,         (27)\n\nuniformly, because beta>=127/128 and c>12/5 gives 3/c^2<25/48<1.\nNo fitted profile, internal child capacity or substituted recurrence is\nused here. Both thresholds tend to (1-1/sigma)/(1+1/sigma)\n=sqrt(2)-1 by their displayed definitions.\n\n## 5. A necessary weak direction for even the scalar payoff\n\nThe exponent L_m in (9) is strictly positive. Indeed (17) implies\n\n    exp(L_m)=beta^2 c^(2m)/(2M_m-1)\n               >beta^2 c^m/2>1.                       (28)\n\nThe last inequality follows already from beta>1/2, c>2 and m>=128.\nThus mu_comp<mu_floor. To check the lower bound in (10), first\n2m/2^m<=1/8 for m>=7, as follows from 2^7>=16*7 and induction.\nSince exp(L_m)<=c^(2m),\n\n    exp(L_m/N)<=c^(1/8)<(5/2)^(1/8)<9/8.                (29)\n\nThe last strict comparison follows by the binomial expansion of\n(1+1/8)^8, retaining enough positive terms to exceed 5/2. Hence\nexp(L_m/N)/(beta c)<9/16 and mu_comp>7/24>1/(2m-1).\n\nSuppose a legal run has V>=K. If mu<mu_1, then already mu<mu_comp.\nOtherwise (25) gives\n\n    q0 >= exp(L_m/N)/(beta c) > 1/(beta c)>a.             (30)\n\nConsequently q0=1-mu ell, and (30) is exactly mu<=mu_comp.\nSubtracting (9) from (7) proves the exact gap formula (10).\n\nThus the background matrix of any competitive member of this static\nfamily must have a sufficiently weak direction in the active space E.\nThis is only a NECESSARY condition. No construction below that floor\nis supplied by the product bound, and mu_comp is not asserted to be a\nsharp threshold for the actual fixed-function family.\n\n## 6. Actual multiple outer sign changes, noncommuting gates and all visits\n\nHere is a concrete run in the resolved class, for EVERY original H_m\nwith m>=128. Let phi_r be the symmetric Huber function with radius\nr=1/100 and define, once and for all,\n\n    F_gate(x,y)=3x^2/8+7y^2/16\n                    +(1/16)phi_r((x+y)/sqrt(2)),\n    X_0=(1,0).                                         (31)\n\nWrite u=(1,1)/sqrt(2), A=diag(3/4,7/8) and\n\n    H=A+(1/16)u u^T=(1/32)[25,1;1,29].                 (32)\n\nThe Hessian in either outer region is A; in the core it is H. Globally\n(3/4)I<=A<=H<=(15/16)I<I. Thus (31) is a fixed convex 1-smooth\nfunction, its unique minimizer is 0, and the true initial squared\ndistance is exactly 1. Its visited Hessians do not commute:\nthe (1,2) entry of A H-H A is -1/256, hence nonzero.\n\nWe prove ALL its region visits, without a finite replay. Abbreviate\n\n    b=d_1=1+beta,\n    k=r/(16sqrt(2))=sqrt(2)/3200,\n    s(lambda)=1-ell lambda, t(lambda)=1-b lambda.\n\nAt X_0 the ridge is strictly positive outer. Its gradient is\n\n    g_0=(3/4+k,k),\n    X_1=(s(3/4)-ell k,-ell k).                         (33)\n\nBy ell>7/5, the first coordinate of X_1 is less than -1/20 and the\nsecond is negative. Hence u^T X_1<-1/(20sqrt(2))<-r: the first actual\nstep lands in the strictly negative outer region. Its gradient is\ng_1=A X_1-k(1,1), and the second actual step gives\n\n    X_2=(t(3/4)[s(3/4)-ell k]+b k,\n                         t(7/8)(-ell k)+b k).          (34)\n\nBoth entries are positive. Since b>19/10, t(3/4)<-17/40, the first\nentry is greater than 17/800. Consequently\nu^T X_2>17/(800sqrt(2))>r, so this query is strictly positive outer.\nThe third physical step is again ell. For lambda=3/4 or 7/8 set\n\n    C(lambda)=-ell s(lambda)t(lambda)+b s(lambda)-ell.\n\nDirectly applying this third step in its now established positive\nouter region yields\n\n    X_3=(s(3/4)^2 t(3/4)+k C(3/4), k C(7/8)).           (35)\n\nAll s,t in (35) are negative. Therefore both entries of X_3 are\nstrictly negative. We next establish a uniform small-norm bound with\nexplicit rational margins. From (16),\n\n    |s(3/4)|<61/1000, |t(3/4)|<1/2,\n    |s(7/8)|<119/500, |t(7/8)|<3/4,\n    ell<283/200, b<2, k<443/1000000.\n\nThese imply\n\n    |C(3/4)| < (283/200)(61/2000)+122/1000+283/200\n                  <1581/1000,\n    |C(7/8)| < (283/200)(357/2000)+238/500+283/200\n                  <2144/1000.\n\nUsing the sum of absolute coordinates to bound the Euclidean norm,\n\n    ||X_3|| < (61/1000)^2/2\n                   +(443/1000000)(3725/1000)\n             =3510675/1000000000 < 9/2500.              (36)\n\nThe slightly larger round margin 9/2500 is retained below; no numerical\nevaluation of the trajectory is being used in (33)--(36).\n\nIt remains to prove that EVERY subsequent actual query and the actual\nterminal sample are in the core. For H_j let R_j be the largest product\nof (h_i-1) over ANY prefix, including the empty prefix. Formula (21) gives\n\n    U_j a c^j=a beta^(2^j-1)<=a<1.\n\nThe physical recursion of H_(j+1) shows that every prefix is either a\nprefix of its first H_j or has product U_j a c^j times a prefix of its\nsecond H_j. Thus R_j=1 for all j. More precisely every NONEMPTY prefix\nhas product at most a: this holds for H_1, is inherited in the first\nchild, and holds in the second child by the displayed inequality.\n\nThe background floor 3/4 exceeds mu_exact, so each ACTUAL step of\n(31) obeys ||X_(i+1)||<=(h_i-1)||X_i||, independently of its current\ngate. The product over the first three steps is U_2=a^2 beta. For any\ni>=3, the suffix product between sample 3 and sample i is therefore\nat most a/U_2=1/(a beta). Since a>2/5 and beta>=127/128,\n\n    ||X_i|| <=||X_3||/(a beta)\n               <(9/2500)(320/127)<1/100=r.              (37)\n\nThis includes i=3. Hence |u^T X_i|<r at EVERY remaining actual sample.\nThere is no circular assumption of future core behavior in (37): its\ncontraction bound was valid for every gate before any visits were known.\n\nThus the complete gate chronology is\n\n    positive outer, negative outer, positive outer,\n                       core, core, ..., core.           (38)\n\nThe terminal position is explicitly specified, without a replay, by\n\n    X_n=[product_(i=3)^(n-1)(I-h_i H)]X_3,               (39)\n\nwith the usual empty-product convention. Equations (33)--(35) and (39)\ngive every actual point and gradient of this one static function.\n\nFor i>=3 all entries of I-h_i H are strictly negative: h_i>=ell>7/5,\nand (7/5)(25/32)>1, (7/5)(29/32)>1. Since both coordinates of X_3\nare negative, multiplication shows that both coordinates of EVERY\nX_i, i>=3, have sign (-1)^i. H has strictly positive entries, so the\nsame is true of both gradient coordinates. Equations (33)--(35) also\ngive g_0 positive, g_1 negative and g_2 positive. Thus there is an actual\ngradient-coordinate sign change at EVERY step, not a relabeling of\ncharacter signs or an alternation of separately chosen functions.\n\nThe gradients are genuinely noncollinear. Let b_vec=k(1,1). In the\nfirst two outer regions,\n\n    g_1=g_0-2b_vec-ell A g_0,\n    det(g_0,g_1)=-3k/2-(ell/8)(3/4+k)k<0.               (40)\n\nMoreover <g_0,g_1><0 while <g_0,g_2>>0, so their normalized pair\ncorrelations are not constant. The switch from A to the noncommuting\nH occurs between actual queried samples 2 and 3. This run is outside\nthe fixed-queried-region hypothesis of C93 and outside the single-\ninitial-sign chronology of C92.\n\nAll original pairs hold by Section 2, and the true radius is 1. Its\nterminal payoff is exactly X_n^T H X_n, with X_n given by (39), and\n\n    0<V_gate<=beta^(2N-2)c^(-2m)<K.                     (41)\n\nTo see strict positivity, H has eigenvalues (27 plus or minus sqrt(5))/32.\nIts smaller eigenvalue exceeds 3/4, and h_i>7/5, so no I-h_i H is\nsingular. Formula (35) is nonzero and H is positive definite. The\nupper comparison follows from (6) and (26). Thus (31) is a real\nchanging-region mechanism, but it supplies NO lower-bound improvement;\nthe same-word accepted global Huber witness is already stronger.\n\n## 7. Attack on the tempting extension below the curvature floor\n\nThe product in (14) is an upper envelope over endpoint contractions.\nIt is not an instruction to switch curvatures independently. A concrete\nfailure occurs on the first two actual steps of H_m. Set mu=1/4 and\npretend that a scalar run starts at x0=1, uses g0=mu x0, and after the\nfirst step uses the upper endpoint curvature with g1=x1. Then\n\n    x1=1-ell/4, 0<x1<x0, and g1=x1>1/4=g0,              (42)\n\nbecause ell<3. These two queries violate monotonicity of the derivative\nof any convex scalar function. Equivalently\n(g0-g1)(x0-x1)<0. They cannot be part of a legal static adversary, even\nthough each chosen scalar curvature separately belongs to [mu,1].\n\nThis attacks the failed positive interpretation of the envelope below\nits proved screen. It does NOT prove that the whole weak-background\nclass is harmless. It also does not assert a multidimensional static\ncounterexample from arbitrary Hessian switching.\n\nThe genuine scalar Huber witness attaining K supplies the opposite\nscope check: it has A=0, lies within (1)--(2), and has value K, which\nexceeds beta^(-2)c^(-2m) for the stated m. What this leaves open, and literature grounding\n\nC94 resolves a changing-queried-region subclass that C93 did not cover:\nan arbitrary number of coupled ridge gates cannot offset a background\nfloor at or above (7). No threshold seed or full remaining-domain exclusion.", "evidence": "# A coupled gap between two original cut energies\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. The only accepted\nmathematical input is the original full certificate identity and full\nprimitive join in fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11. Every additional claim used below is\nproved here. Earlier drafts are context, not correctness premises.\n\nThe new interface is quantitative: a negative two-step prefix cut cannot\nbe supported by an arbitrarily small repaired last-cut energy. The\noriginal cross rows of M and their actual coefficient signs impose an\nexplicit gap. Keeping the earlier first-cut budget gives a closed,\npiecewise solution of the resulting two-energy optimization, including\nzero and saturated energies. This is not a sufficiency test for the\nentire primitive cone.\n\nSection6.1 strengthens this with an exact sum-of-squares identity and\nclassifies its ENTIRE equality face in the original twenty-sign cone.\nThat face has explicit full certificates, but all its capacities are\nstrictly below77/10<193/25. The threshold equality boundary is therefore\ndecided, including its remaining endpoint regimes, without reversal.\n\nThe original threshold seed and exact-profile target remain open. In\nparticular, this draft does NOT claim that the excluded energy strip\ncontains a point passing every previously published scalar/triangle\ntest. That additional independence question is separated from the\nfull-cone exclusion actually proved here.\n\n## 1. Original space and notation\n\nInitially assume\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v), a,b,c>0, v>=0,\n    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,\n    C=1+sum h=4+a+b+c+v,\n    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, Hpre=2+a+b, Apre=1+a+b,\n    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t,\n    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t),\n    Rcut=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2.                 (1)\n\nThe repaired-cut results assume b*t<=1, so ell>=0. No division by ell\nwill appear in the final test. When comparing with the earlier positive\nexcess regime, write d=v-1; that comparison explicitly assumes d>0.\n\nLet P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j, s=P4=C-1, e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, and let e_i be the\nfive original standard coordinate columns. Set\n\n    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=D+sym(LT).\n\nFull feasibility means Lambda_ii=0, all twenty Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j,\nL^T e=omega, and M positive semidefinite on ALL of R^5. Here\nsym(Z)=(Z+Z^T)/2. The accepted identity is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                                   -s(s+1)||g4||^2.\n                                                               (2)\n\nThis is a polynomial identity on every formal gradient and value after\nthe GD recursion x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i is substituted. A formal substitution\nneed not itself be a legal trajectory; any use of that distinction is\nexplicit below.\n\nThe flow gives e^T M e=0: Te=e+P and\n2sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2, whereas\ne^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2. PSD forces Me=0 by applying nonnegativity to\n(e+z*q)^T M(e+z*q) for both signs of z. It also gives\n\n    |x^T M y|^2 <= (x^T M x)(y^T M y).                 (3)\n\nIf one energy is zero, the mixed term is zero, by the same polynomial\nargument. No positive-definiteness assumption is needed.\n\nDefine the two ORIGINAL cut columns\n\n    jcut=e0+e1, kcut=e3+e4,\n    S=jcut^T M jcut, Tcut=kcut^T M kcut, r=jcut^T M kcut,\n    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,\n    Bmass=Hpre*B0+beta*B1.                              (4)\n\nThus S,Tcut,B0,B1>=0 and |r|<=sqrt(S*Tcut). None of the formal gradients\nare identified, even in the boundary v=0.\n\n## 2. Exact cost of the repaired last-cut geometry\n\nConsider scalar base and auxiliary data, with saturated values\nf_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 in each array:\n\n    xB=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t),        gB=(1,1,1,-t,-t),\n    xA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,-a*b*t),\n    gA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*t,-a*b*t).                        (5)\n\nBoth arrays obey the ORIGINAL four steps, because c=(1+v)*t. Their\nnormals x_i-g_i are\n\n    yB=(Hpre,beta,0,-v*t,0),\n    yA=(0,0,0,-a*b*v*t,0).\n\nFor saturated values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)*y_j. Take their orthogonal sum\nwith weights ell and mu: positions and gradients are\n(sqrt(ell)*xB_i,sqrt(mu)*xA_i) and\n(sqrt(ell)*gB_i,sqrt(mu)*gA_i), and values are ell*fB_i+mu*fA_i.\n\nHere is the complete list of nonzero finite residuals, with repeated\nindices denoting their original ordered pairs:\n\n    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre,\n    Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,\n    Q03=-a*b*u*mu*v*t,\n    Q23=-a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.                             (6)\n\nEvery other ordered finite residual is zero. In particular Q13=0:\nthe base residual -v*t*(1+t) and the auxiliary residual\na^2*b*v*t*(1-b*t) cancel after weighting. For Q03, the positive cost\nis v*t*mu*[ell+a*b*(1+a*b*t)]=a*b*u*mu*v*t. For Q23 it is\nv*t*mu*[ell+a^2*b^2*mu]=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t. These identities also\nhold when ell=0 or v=0.\n\nThe zero optimum jet can be added. Residuals directed toward it vanish.\nThe opposite residuals are the negatives of <G_i,X_i-G_i>; at targets\n0,1,3 these inner products are respectively ell*Hpre, ell*beta, and\nv*t^2*(ell+mu*a^2*b^2), all nonnegative. The other two are zero.\n\nThese are samples of one actual global convex differentiable function\nwith 1-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer. Indeed take Kphys to be the\nconvex hull of zero and the five displayed gradient vectors, and set\n\n    Fphys(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).\n\nThe residual checks say precisely that X_i-G_i is an outward normal\nat G_i. Completing the square makes the unique maximizer the Euclidean\nprojection onto Kphys. Adding the two projection normal inequalities\ngives ||G(X)-G(Y)||^2<=<G(X)-G(Y),X-Y>, hence the projection is\n1-Lipschitz. Optimality at X and X+z bounds\nFphys(X+z)-Fphys(X)-<G(X),z> between zero and ||z||^2, proving\ndifferentiability. Convexity follows from the maximum of affine\nfunctions, and Fphys>=0 with Fphys(0)=0. This construction also covers\ndegenerate Kphys at ell=0 or v=0. It is one static function.\n\nFor any saturated-value data the squared-distance telescope in (2) is\n\n    V_h=||X0||^2-||X4+s*G4||^2.\n\nHere X4=G4 and ell+mu*a^2*b^2=a^2*b*beta. Consequently the energy is\nexactly Rcut in (1). The full identity (2), with all costs (6), gives\n\n Rcut=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*gA^T M gA+ell*mu*Bmass\n          +a*b*u*mu*v*t*Lambda03\n          +a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t*Lambda23.                 (7)\n\nThe scalar column gA in this formula is the five-entry column in (5).\nEvery summand is nonnegative for a feasible array. In particular\n\n    Rcut>=0,\n    ell*[mu^2*Tcut+mu*Bmass] <= Rcut.                  (8)\n\nNotice the cross-term-containing energy that has NOT disappeared:\n\n    gA=u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut+a*b*e,\n    gA^T M gA=||u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut||_M^2.    (9)\n\nHere ||z||_M^2 means z^T M z. Formula (9) will delimit the relaxation\nsolved below; it is not set to zero in a claimed full certificate.\n\n## 3. First-cut and first-row budgets, retained simultaneously\n\nSet\n\n    eta=b/(1+w+v), Fcut=A^2-C^2*eta^2.                  (10)\n\nThe scalar function with derivative clamp(x,[-eta,1]) and minimum zero,\nstarted at A, has gradients (1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta) for this word. Its\nendpoint equals its gradient and its primitive energy is Fcut. Its\ntwenty residuals are nonpositive, so (2) and Me=0 give\n\n    Fcut>=0,  S<=Fcut/(1+eta)^2.                       (11)\n\nThere is a second, sometimes stronger budget from the initial lifted\ncut. The original diagonal is M00=1-a^2-a*sum_(j>0)Lambda0j. The\nconstant-kernel row moment is sum_(j>0)P_j*Lambda0j=2u. Therefore full\nfeasibility implies 0<a<1. The scalar bound C*eta<=A and C>A give\na*eta<1. Put\n\n    delta=u^2-2,\n    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2,\n    chi=1/nu+1/(1+eta)^2.                              (12)\n\nThe lifted-cut accounting, reproved in Appendix A, is\n\n Erow=nu*(M00+a*(Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40))\n       +(1+eta)^2*M11\n       +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (13)\n\nThe construction and identity (13) need only 0<a<1, a*eta<1 and the\ncurrent positive a,b,c with v>=0; the stronger first-row gap from the\nappendix has its original additional hypotheses. PSD and weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz applied to sqrt(M00),sqrt(M11) show\n\n    S<=(sqrt(M00)+sqrt(M11))^2<=chi*Erow.\n\nDefine the completely explicit nonnegative budget\n\n    Gcap=min(Fcut/(1+eta)^2, chi*Erow).                 (14)\n\nThus S<=Gcap. A negative Fcut or Erow already excludes feasibility.\nNo choice between the two actual functions changes the multiplier\narray: both inequalities must hold for the SAME original M and Lambda.\n\nFor d=v-1>0 retain the original quantitative result2948d4cd3660413f\nexactly: with Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,\nWrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow), and a>sqrt(2)-1,\n\n u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu),\n Erow>=nu*[u*delta/(sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)\n                                 +sqrt(Qenergy))]^2>0. (15)\n\nHere 0<=Qenergy<1 is necessary. If additionally a*b<=1 and c*d<=1,\nthe strict consequences remain Erow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and\nb<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)). Appendix A supplies their full proof,\nincluding Qenergy=0. No reversal or extension to other endpoint regimes\nis inferred from (15).\n\n## 4. The original cross rows price a prefix deficit\n\nLet\n\n    Dprefix=N^2*b^2-A^2.                               (16)\n\nThis is the negative of the primitive energy of the actual two-step\nprefix (u,beta), on the scalar cut started at A with gradients\n(1,1,-b). To apply the full identity, extend these data FORMALLY by\nconstant gradient -b through the last two updates and affine function\nvalues. The extension need not be legal; this is a substitution in (2).\nIts full primitive energy equals the prefix energy A^2-N^2*b^2, as\ndirect substitution or telescoping the two constant-gradient updates\nshows.\n\nThe formal gradient column is beta*jcut-b*e. The only negative\nresiduals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta. The only positive residuals are\n\n    Q03=Q13=w*b*beta,\n    Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta.\n\nAll remaining ordered residuals are zero. Hence (2) gives the EXACT\nfull-array identity\n\n Dprefix=b*beta*[w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)\n                         +(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14)]\n             -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).           (17)\n\nIndependently, expand the original rows0 and1 against kcut. Since\nT e0=u*e-a*e0 and\nT e1=beta*e-beta*e0-b*e1, one obtains\n\n 2*e0^T M kcut=a*B0-Lambda03-(v+1)*Lambda04,\n 2*e1^T M kcut=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(v+1)*Lambda14.\n\nThus\n\n w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14)\n    =w*(Apre*B0+b*B1-2r)\n                         -v*(w-1)*(Lambda04+Lambda14).\n\nSubstitution in (17) yields\n\n Dprefix/beta=(b*w*Apre-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*r-beta*S\n                -u*Lambda20-b*v*(w-1)*(Lambda04+Lambda14). (18)\n\nThe two subtracted terms have their original nonnegative signs. Define\n\n    kappa=max((b*w*Apre-u)/Hpre, b^2*w/beta)>0,\n    tau=kappa/mu, zeta=b*w/mu.                          (19)\n\nSince B0,B1>=0, their two positive-or-signed contributions in (18) are\nat most kappa*Bmass. This remains valid if the first coefficient is\nnegative. No outgoing terminal coefficient was discarded before its\ncross-row contribution was accounted for.\n\nIf ell>0 put Ecut=Rcut/ell. Combining (8), (18), and (3), with\nx=sqrt(S), y=mu*sqrt(Tcut), gives\n\n Dprefix<=beta*tau*Ecut-beta*tau*y^2\n                       +2*beta*zeta*x*y-beta^2*x^2,\n    0<=y^2<=Ecut, 0<=x^2<=Gcap.                       (20)\n\nThis is the new simultaneous energy/sign interface. Dropping r would\nlose its mixed term. Replacing small positive energy by a zero row would\nalso be invalid; (20) does neither.\n\n## 5. Complete exact decision of the two-energy optimization\n\nFor Earg,Garg>=0 define Psi(Earg,Garg) as follows. The fixed positive constants\nbeta,tau,zeta are those in (19).\n\nIf zeta^2<=beta*tau, set\n\n    Psi(Earg,Garg)=beta*tau*Earg.                               (21)\n\nIf zeta^2>beta*tau, set\n\n Psi(Earg,Garg)=beta*tau*Earg+beta*(zeta^2/tau-beta)*Garg\n                         when Garg<=tau^2*Earg/zeta^2;\n          2*beta*zeta*sqrt(Earg*Garg)-beta^2*Garg\n                         when tau^2*Earg/zeta^2<=Garg\n                                      <=zeta^2*Earg/beta^2;\n          zeta^2*Earg       when Garg>=zeta^2*Earg/beta^2.       (22)\n\nAt the shared endpoints the expressions agree. These formulas include\nEarg=0 or Garg=0 without division by either energy. In particular Psi(0,Garg)=0\nand Psi(Earg,0)=beta*tau*Earg.\n\nHere is a direct proof that Psi is exactly the maximum in (20). For\nfixed y, the unconstrained optimal x is zeta*y/beta, truncated at\nsqrt(Garg). Completing the square in x gives\n\n -beta^2*(x-zeta*y/beta)^2+(zeta^2-beta*tau)*y^2.\n\nIf the last coefficient is nonpositive, the maximum is zero, attained\nat x=y=0, which proves (21). Otherwise this expression increases along\nthe unconstrained x optimum until y reaches sqrt(Earg) or x reaches\nsqrt(Garg). On the latter boundary the concave quadratic in y is maximized\nat min(zeta*sqrt(Garg)/tau,sqrt(Earg)); this value is at least the entry point\nbeta*sqrt(Garg)/zeta because zeta^2>beta*tau. These alternatives give the\nthree cases in (22), respectively. They also prove attainment in the\nclosed rectangle, rather than only an upper bound.\n\nThe final division-free necessary condition is\n\n                  ell*Dprefix <= Psi(Rcut,ell*Gcap).   (23)\n\nFor ell>0 it follows from (20) because Psi is homogeneous of degree one\nin (Earg,Garg). For ell=0 the geometry still gives Rcut>=0, and (23) reads\n0<=Psi(Rcut,0)=beta*tau*Rcut. It is deliberately vacuous about the\nprefix at that boundary. There is no cancellation of a zero factor.\n\nThe maximization just solved is the stated TWO-ENERGY relaxation, not\nthe full original feasibility problem. For example (7) retains the\nadditional energy (9), as well as two priced original multipliers.\nAttaining (21) or (22) alone need not satisfy those terms, the remaining\nrows, calibration, or all twenty signs.\n\n## 6. A concrete gap strip in the remaining bc<=1 branch\n\nThere is a particularly simple specialization, with NO ordering of\nthe endpoint steps. If b*c<=1, then\n\n (b*w*Apre-u)/Hpre-b^2*w/beta\n                       =u*(b*c-1)/(beta*Hpre)<=0.\n\nConsequently kappa=b^2*w/beta. Put\n\n    gamma=w/mu=w*(1+v)/(w+v)>=1.\n\nWhen v>0, gamma>1. Formula (22) simplifies to\n\n Psi(Earg,Garg)=b^2*gamma*Earg+beta^2*(gamma-1)*Garg\n                           if beta^2*Garg<=b^2*Earg;\n          2*b*beta*gamma*sqrt(Earg*Garg)-beta^2*Garg\n                           if b^2*Earg<=beta^2*Garg<=b^2*gamma^2*Earg;\n          b^2*gamma^2*Earg    if beta^2*Garg>=b^2*gamma^2*Earg.  (24)\n\nFor v=0 all three expressions have the common maximum b^2*Earg, as\nalso supplied by (21). In all cases Psi(Earg,Garg)<=b^2*gamma^2*Earg. Thus\n\n                Rcut>=ell*Dprefix/(b^2*gamma^2)         (25)\n\nwhenever b*c<=1. This is substantive when ell>0 and Dprefix>0. It\nexcludes the entire full-cone region\n\n C>=193/25, a,b,c>0, v>=0, b*t<1, b*c<=1, Dprefix>0,\n             0<=Rcut<ell*Dprefix/(b^2*gamma^2).         (26)\n\nThe capacity is variable; no upper-capacity slice or endpoint square\nis used. Every full-support array is excluded there, not just a center,\nsparse ansatz, chosen rank or supplied covariance fiber.\n\nA simpler threshold strip can be stated without Dprefix. If b>=1,\nb*c<=1, v>0 and ell>0, then b*gamma=b*w/mu<beta. Also N=A+beta and\n\n Dprefix/beta^2\n    =b^2+2*b^2*A/beta+(b^2-1)*A^2/beta^2\n    >=1+A=3+a,\n\nbecause beta<=2b. Here Dprefix>0. Therefore every feasible array in\nthis regime obeys the STRICT bound\n\n                         Rcut>ell*(3+a).              (27)\n\nIn particular the whole strip 0<=Rcut<=ell*(3+a) is excluded at every\nC>=193/25, with v>0 free and no assumption that either endpoint lies\nin a previously excluded square or prism. This is a resolved energy\nstrip of the original cone. It does not assert that this strip survives\nevery older scalar witness family. No excluded square/prism is a premise.\n\nAt Rcut=0 and ell>0 the general result (23), even without b*c<=1,\nforces N*b<=A. Thus saturation of the repaired last-cut energy has an\nexplicit prefix-cut consequence, with its quantitative neighborhood\ncontrolled by (21)--(23). This does not assert arbitrary prefix or tail\nheredity away from that energy budget.\n\n### 6.1. An exact remainder and the entire equality face\n\nThe simple bound (25) has more structure than its relaxed derivation\nshows. Assume b*c<=1 and set\n\n    Kgap=b^2*gamma^2,\n    C03=a*b*u*mu*v*t, C23=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t,\n    Ngap=beta*jcut+b*w*kcut,\n    Agap=u*e0-a*beta*jcut-a*b*mu*kcut,\n    Gap=Kgap*Rcut-ell*Dprefix.\n\nEquations(7) and(18), WITHOUT dropping their extra terms, give the\nfollowing exact original-array identity:\n\n Gap=ell*||Ngap||_M^2+Kgap*mu*||Agap||_M^2\n   +ell*[b^2*w*Hpre*(gamma-1)+u*(1-b*c)]*B0\n   +ell*b^2*w*beta*(gamma-1)*B1\n   +ell*beta*u*Lambda20\n   +ell*beta*b*v*c*(Lambda04+Lambda14)\n   +Kgap*C03*Lambda03+Kgap*C23*Lambda23.                (33)\n\nFor a coefficient check, the terms involving S,r,Tcut are\nell*[beta^2*S+2*beta*b*w*r+b^2*w^2*Tcut], exactly the first square.\nThe B1 coefficient is ell*b^2*w*beta*(w/mu-1). The B0 coefficient\nuses beta*(b*w*Apre-u)-b^2*w*Hpre=u*(b*c-1), giving the displayed\nvalue. The auxiliary energy and all remaining signs are unchanged from\n(7),(18). This proves(33) without an optimization or a division by ell.\nEvery summand is nonnegative on the stated domain, including b*c=1.\n\nSuppose now ell>0 and v>0, still with a,b,c>0 and b*c<=1. Then\ngamma>1 and C03,C23>0. If Gap=0, every summand in(33) vanishes, so\n\n B0=B1=Lambda20=Lambda04=Lambda14=Lambda03=Lambda23=0,\n                     M*Ngap=M*Agap=0.                 (34)\n\nZero PSD energy implies the zero column even when M is singular. The\noriginal first cross row gives e0^T M kcut=0 from(34). Moreover\n\n                 Agap+a*Ngap=u*e0+a*b*v*t*kcut.\n\nTaking its M inner product with kcut gives a*b*v*t*Tcut=0. Every\nfactor except Tcut is strictly positive, so Tcut=0 and M*kcut=0.\nNow (34) gives M*jcut=M*e0=0, hence M*e1=M*e2=0 as well, the last\nusing Me=0. Formula(7) gives Rcut=0, and Gap=0 with ell>0 then gives\nDprefix=0. Therefore\n\n    Gap=0 implies Rcut=Dprefix=0, M e0=M e1=M e2=M kcut=0. (35)\n\nIn particular (25) is STRICT when v>0, ell>0 and Dprefix>0. This\nrules out the equality boundary in(26), not merely its open part.\n\nThere is an exact classification of the remaining case in(35). Under\nthe same positive-parameter assumptions a full certificate with Gap=0\nexists if and only if\n\n a=sqrt(2)-1, A=1+sqrt(2),\n b>0, b*N=A, N=A+1+b,\n 0<v<=sqrt(2),\n c>0, c*C=N*(1+v), C=N+(1+v)+c.                        (36)\n\nHere the b and c equations each have exactly one positive solution.\nEvery word in(36) in fact has b*c<1 and b*t<1, so it lies in the\ndomain being classified. This is an existential classification of the\nfull cone on this equality face, not a condition on one supplied array.\n\nTo prove necessity, (34) and M*jcut=0 imply Lambda13=0 by the second\ncross-row formula in Section4. Thus all edges between {0,1} and {3,4}\nvanish in both directions. Restrict Lambda to {0,1,2}. Its flow at\n0,1 is still -2u,-2beta, so its terminal flow is 2(u+beta). The first\ntwo rows of its original three-gradient primitive matrix are exactly\nthe corresponding rows of M and therefore zero. This can also be\nchecked entrywise: the rows retain Lambda01,Lambda02,Lambda10,Lambda12,\nLambda20,Lambda21; the vanished cross edges contribute nothing, while\nTe0=u*e-a*e0 and Te1=beta*e-beta*e0-b*e1 eliminate any row2 outgoing\nedge to a later index. The constant-gradient scalar form is zero by\nthe restricted flow, so its remaining diagonal is zero too. Thus the\nentire prefix matrix is zero.\n\nEvaluate that zero matrix on the prefix unit-quadratic column\n(1,-a,a*b). The direct telescope gives 1-N^2*a^2*b^2=0, hence\nN*a*b=1. From Dprefix=0 one has N*b=A. Therefore a*A=1, yielding\na=sqrt(2)-1 and the first two lines of(36).\n\nSubstitute N*a*b=1 into(1). It gives\n\n                Rcut=a^2*b*beta*(N^2-C^2*t^2).\n\nHence Rcut=0 forces C*t=N, which is c*C=N*(1+v). Finally the actual\nunit-quadratic column for all four steps is\n(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*(v-1)). It obeys Tp=e, so the same direct\ntelescope and PSD give\n\n 0<=1-[C*a*b*c*(v-1)]^2=1-(v^2-1)^2=v^2*(2-v^2).\n\nSince v>0 this is v<=sqrt(2). This argument allows v<=1 and does not\nimport the positive-d hypothesis of the earlier strict first-row gap.\n\nFor sufficiency, there is an explicit full original array. Put\nJ=N^2-1 and Kjoin=(C/(1+v))^2. The nonzero entries are precisely\n\n Lambda01=2,                 Lambda10=2*(1+u),\n Lambda12=J*b,               Lambda21=J,\n Lambda24=(Kjoin-1)*c,\n Lambda32=(Kjoin-1)*v,       Lambda42=Kjoin-1,\n Lambda34=2*Kjoin+(Kjoin-1)*v,\n Lambda43=2*Kjoin*(1+v).                               (37)\n\nAll other eleven ordered entries are zero. The FULL remainder is\n\n          M=Kjoin*(2-v^2)*(e3-e4)*(e3-e4)^T>=0.         (38)\n\nHere is a complete way to audit (37)--(38), using the full primitive\njoin identity already accepted in04219554e41d9750, Section11. A one-step\nword z has multipliers Lambda01=2, Lambda10=2(1+z) and remainder\n(2-z^2)*(e0-e1)*(e0-e1)^T, as substitution in the original flow and\nmatrix shows. It is PSD for 0<=z<=sqrt(2), and is zero for z=sqrt(2).\nThe empty word has zero energy and remainder. For child capacities\nAl,Ar and bridge excess q with q*(Al+Ar+q)=Al*Ar, the exact identity is\n\n V_join=V_left+Kj*V_right-(Kj-1)*E_PP,\n Kj=((Al+Ar+q)/Ar)^2,\n E_PP=sum_j right_j*Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+q*Q_(p,z)\n                                +sum_j right_j*Q_(j,z).\n\nThe indices p,z are the actual bridge and terminal queries, and j runs\nover the right child's nonterminal queries. Every added coefficient is\nnonnegative and the full remainder is the original-coordinate sum of\nthe left remainder and Kj times the right remainder. Applying this\nidentity first to (sqrt(2)), empty, bridge b gives the first four\nentries of(37) and zero remainder. Applying it to that two-step prefix,\nthe one-step word(v), and bridge c gives exactly the other five entries\nand(38), on all five distinct formal coordinates. Thus (37) satisfies\nthe original flow and all twenty signs, not only a reduced matrix test.\nEquations(36) give Rcut=Dprefix=0, so Gap=0.\n\nFor completeness, b*c=A*(1+v)/C<1: N>A+1=(A-1)*A and 1+v<=A,\nso N>(A-1)*(1+v), and C>N+(1+v)>A*(1+v). Also b*t=A/C<1.\n\nEvery capacity in(36) is strictly below77/10. Let\nOmega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2, the positive root of\nz^2-(x+y)*z-x*y=0. It is increasing in each positive argument, either\nby this displayed formula or by comparing that polynomial at a root.\nWe have A<29/12 because sqrt(2)<17/12. At z=201/50,\n\n z^2-(29/12+1)*z-29/12=131/15000>0,\n\nso N=Omega(A,1)<201/50. Since 1+v<=A,\nC=Omega(N,1+v)<Omega(201/50,29/12). At z=77/10,\n\n z^2-(201/50+29/12)*z-(201/50)*(29/12)=19/1500>0.\n\nThus C<77/10<193/25. Consequently EVERY full threshold certificate in\nthe whole domain ell>0,v>0,b*c<=1 must have Gap>0, including Dprefix=0.\nThe equality face is completely decided and has explicit low-capacity\nfull certificates; no point on it is an improving threshold seed.\n\n## 7. Relation to full calibration and the uncovered freedom\n\nFor d=v-1>0, let p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T. Then Tp=e, and\ntelescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 proves\n\n                  p^T M p=Qenergy.                    (28)\n\nThe columns e,e0,jcut,kcut,p form a basis: any linear combination of\nthe first four has equal entries at indices3 and4, whereas p3-p4 is\n-a*b*c*v, which is nonzero. Thus full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the\nFOUR-by-FOUR Gram matrix of e0,jcut,kcut,p. All mixed entries matter.\n\nIn particular (7) retains the full quadratic expression (9), not just\nS and Tcut. The entry r is retained through (18)--(20), but those\ninequalities do not determine e0^T M kcut or kcut^T M p. The earlier\nrow0 identity prices e0^T M p by actual incoming coefficients; it does\nnot determine these other entries. A relaxed optimizer from (22) can\nfail these remaining joint requirements.\n\nHere is a complete coordinate check showing that no skew/sign freedom\nhas been silently removed. For positive steps choose P3<rho<s and put\n\n phi_i=1+P_i, weight_i=2h_i/(rho-P_i) for i<4,\n weight_4=2s/(s-rho), W=sum weight_i,\n Lambda^c_ij=phi_i*weight_i*weight_j/W for i!=j.\n\nThe sums sum weight_i*(P_i-rho)=0 and\nsum phi_i*weight_i=(1+rho)*W show that this strictly positive center\nhas the required flow and constant-kernel row moments. They do NOT\nshow PSD. Let E_diff have columns e_i-e4, i0 through3, and F_null\nhave columns e_j-(1-P_j/s)e0-(P_j/s)e4, j1 through3. All real\nflow/kernel completions are exactly\n\n    L=L^c+E_diff*X*F_null^T, X any real4by3 matrix.     (29)\n\nIndeed the difference annihilates e and P on the right and e on the\nleft, and these two displayed bases describe precisely those spaces.\nThe twenty signs remain the simultaneous inequalities\n\n    (E_diff*X*F_null^T)_ij<=Lambda^c_ij, every i!=j.    (30)\n\nLet B_pick=(e0,e1,e2,e3), Rmap=F_null^T*T*B_pick and\nK_c=B_pick^T M^c B_pick. Then\n\n K=K_c+sym(X*Rmap),\n M=[[K,-K*1],[-1^T*K,1^T*K*1]],\n q=(p0-p4,p1-p4,p2-p4,p3-p4)^T,\n q^T K q=Qenergy.                                     (31)\n\nFull M>=0 is equivalent to K>=0. Since T^(-1)P=e-p,\nker(F_null^T T)=span(e,p), so Rmap has rank3 and kernel span(q), with\nq nonzero. For any proposed calibrated K put H=K-K_c and\n\n Szero=[q*(Hq)^T-(Hq)*q^T]/(q^T q).\n\nThen Szero*q=-Hq, and every real completion is\n\n X=(H+Szero+Z)*Rmap^T*(Rmap*Rmap^T)^(-1),\n                 Z^T=-Z, Z*q=0.                      (32)\n\nMultiplication by Rmap verifies this identity; conversely X*Rmap-H\nis skew and has value -Hq on q. The free skew space on q-perp is\nthree-dimensional. None of its three parameters, none of the twenty\nsigns, and no original gradient coordinate has been deleted. At\nQenergy=0, K>=0 forces Kq=0 without division by that energy.\n\nThe new gap excludes every choice in (29)--(32) over the region (26).\nOutside it, a calibrated PSD K, a positive center, and an optimizer of\nthe cut relaxation are still three separate requirements. No full\ntwenty-sign/high-capacity completion has been produced or excluded.\nAt zero physical steps one uses the original full matrix and (7)--(23);\nthe positive-step coordinate paragraph and its independent p column\nare not invoked. In particular v=0 never identifies g3 with g4 in a\nformal certificate.\n\n## 8. Direct-proof and counterexample screens\n\nThe exact endpoint-saturation route was recalled and not re-proved as\na new result; shared6fbfca5386425d02 already handles its conditional\nthree-step interface. The present mechanism instead retains a positive\ncut energy and its actual cross rows.\n\nA first attempt, on positive a,b,c,d with a<sqrt(2)-1 and b*c<=1,\nuses row0 and the quadratic energy after dropping a nonnegative incoming\nterm. Writing m=M00 and Dsmall=2-u^2>0, it gives\nu*(m+Dsmall)<=2*sqrt(m*Qenergy). This particular bound minimizes at\nm=Dsmall and yields Qenergy>=u^2*(2-u^2), equivalently\nC*b*c*d<=a+2. It is the known scalar prefix-clipping condition, and is\nnot counted as a new obstruction. No redundancy of the entire row0\nsystem is inferred from this one optimized relaxation.\nAn attempted rational boundary example for independence of the new\ngap failed the pre-existing partial-return inequality C*b*c*d<=2+a;\nit is not a survivor or a seed. No numerical/model run was made.\n\nThe identities were checked against ell=0, Rcut=0, S=0, Tcut=0,\nQenergy=0, b*c=1 and v=0. The general kappa in (19) retains the\nb*c>1 case. The smaller kappa and gamma formulas are used only when\nb*c<=1. All actual residual signs were enumerated in (6); the prefix\nextension in Section4 is explicitly formal, and its positive residuals\nare retained rather than asserted legal. The solved optimization is\nlabeled a relaxation throughout.\n\nTargeted fact_search found no accepted result for this interface.\nMatlas searches returned unrelated matrix/optimization statements;\nweb fallback recalled the already audited composition literature. No\nexternal theorem is imported and no new literature-based branch is\nrejected. The proof uses only the accepted primitive identity plus\nthe complete elementary derivations here.\n\nThis round gives the exact cross-row/cut optimization (21)--(23), the\nfull-cone gap strip (26)--(27), the exact nonnegative remainder (33),\nand the full equality-face classification with explicit original arrays\n(36)--(38). That face has capacity strictly below77/10, so it is absent\nat the threshold193/25. The remaining three-vector energy and full\ncalibration/sign interface are explicit in (9),(28)--(32). The draft does not\ngive an actual threshold seed, a full cover of the remaining scalar\ndomain, general reversal, or a verified original-target result.\n\n## Appendix A. Reproduced first-row proof\n\nThe following proof text is reproduced to retain the original hypotheses\nand quantitative bounds of2948d4cd3660413f. It is part of this joint\nunverified draft, not a citation to an accepted predecessor. The current\nmain text separately notes where its accounting construction extends\nto v>=0; the strict positive-excess conclusions remain exactly scoped.\n\n### 1. Statement and notation\n\nWrite a positive-excess four-step word as\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),    a,b,c,d>0,\n    C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d.\n\nSuppose it admits the full primitive certificate of accepted fact\n04219554e41d9750. Suppose additionally that a>sqrt(2)-1. Define\n\n    u=1+a,             delta=u^2-2,\n    H=3+c+d,           eta=b/H,\n    R=a+3+1/a,         k=u*delta/a,\n    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=R-k*eta-C^2*eta^2,\n    Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,\n    Wrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d),\n    Krow=max(u,a*Wrow).\n\nFull feasibility forces a<1, a*eta<1, nu>0, and\n0<=Qenergy<1. The following quantitative necessary condition holds:\n\n    u*delta <= Krow*Erow/nu + 2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).          (A)\n\nIn particular,\n\n    Erow >= nu * [u*delta /\n          (sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)+sqrt(Qenergy))]^2 > 0.  (B)\n\nThus equality in the previously used lifted middle-cut condition Erow>=0\nis impossible throughout this initial-step regime. A version avoiding\nsquare roots is\n\n    Erow>=0,\n    [max(u*delta*nu-Krow*Erow,0)]^2 <= 4*Qenergy*Erow*nu.     (C)\n\nIf a*b<=1 and c*d<=1, the simpler strict necessary inequality is\n\n    Erow > nu*delta^2/u^2,                                 (D)\n\nequivalently\n\n    b < (a+3)*(C-a-2) / ((a+1)*(C+a+2)).                (E)\n\nThe last two product assumptions are explicit hypotheses of this\nsimplification. They are not silently declared lossless for the\noriginal problem. Conditions(A)--(C) do not require them. The separate\nassumption a,b,c,d>0 is also retained explicitly; no assertion about a\nzero step or a step at most one is being imported from an unverified\ncompactness draft.\n\n### 2. Original formal certificate and its constant kernel\n\nPut P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_4=C-1, and let e be the five-entry all-ones\ncolumn. Write e_i for the i-th original standard coordinate of R^5,\nwith indices0 through4. Let T have diagonal1, entry h_t at(i,t) for t<i, and zero\nentries above the diagonal. The original twenty multipliers satisfy\n\n    Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,\n    L=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L^T e=omega,\n    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0 on ALL of R^5.               (1)\n\nThe identity in accepted04219554e41d9750 is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                      =sum_(i,j) M_ij <g_i,g_j>,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)\n          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i.                                (2)\n\nSince Te=e+P and sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2,\n\n    e^TDe=-s^2-sum h_i^2,\n    omega^TTe=s^2+sum h_i^2,\n    e^TMe=0.\n\nFor every real v, the nonnegative quadratic(e+t*v)^T M(e+t*v)\nhas zero constant coefficient. Considering both signs of t forces\nv^TMe=0, hence Me=0. The initial row of this identity is\n\n    sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j=2h0.                        (3)\n\nTo check(3) without any suppressed flow condition, direct expansion\ngives(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 for i<4. At i=0,\nP0=0 and(LP)_0=-sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j.\n\nLet Aout=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j and m=M00. Its original diagonal is\n\n    m=1-a^2-a*Aout.                                    (4)\n\nIndeed the first column of T is(1,u,u,u,u)^T, so(LT)_00\n=(1-u)Aout=-a*Aout. Since m,Aout>=0 and a>0, (4) gives\na<=1. Equality would give m=Aout=0, contradicting(3). Thus a<1.\n\nFor any positive semidefinite real matrix, the two-vector inequality\n\n    |v^T M w|^2 <= (v^T M v)*(w^T M w)                 (5)\n\nfollows by applying nonnegativity to(v+t*w)^T M(v+t*w).\nIf w^T M w=0, the linear coefficient must vanish; otherwise minimizing\nin t proves(5). This argument does not assume positive definiteness.\n\n### 3. Two exact scalar energies\n\nHere clamp(x,[-eta,1]) means min(1,max(-eta,x)).\nThe scalar function with this derivative and minimum0\nhas the actual trajectory\n\n    x=(2+a,1,-b,-(2+d)*eta,-eta),\n    g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).\n\nHere b=(3+c+d)*eta. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and\n1-Lipschitz. At every displayed sample its value is\nf_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. The first step gives x1=1; the other three GD\nupdates follow directly from the displayed relation for b.\n\nFor any GD data with saturated values\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, telescoping the squared distances gives\n\n    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2.                        (6)\n\nIn detail, the first two sums in(2) reduce to\n2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2-2s*f4;\nthe first two terms telescope to||x0||^2-||x4||^2.\nSubstitution of f4 then gives(6). The scalar trajectory has x4=g4,\nso its energy is(2+a)^2-C^2*eta^2. By(1)--(2) it is nonnegative.\nConsequently\n\n    C*eta<=2+a,\n    a*eta<=a*(2+a)/C<3/5<1,                           (7)\n\nbecause 0<a<1 and C>5. This proves nu>0.\n\nNext define the actual unit-quadratic gradient column\n\n    p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T.\n\nIt satisfies Tp=e. Since Le=0,\n\n    p^T M p=p^T D p=1-C^2*p4^2=Qenergy.               (8)\n\nFor the telescoping equality, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i gives\nh_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2; the terminal diagonal\nthen supplies-s(s+2)p4^2. Full PSD gives Qenergy>=0.\nAll a,b,c,d are positive, so Qenergy<1.\n\n### 4. Complete lifted-cut data and its exact multiplier cost\n\nLet U=(1,0), V=(0,1) be orthonormal physical vectors. Set\n\n    z=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V.\n\nThen\n\n    ||z||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a,\n    <U,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*eta.\n\nUse the five two-dimensional data\n\n    X=(U+u*z, U, -b*U, -(2+d)*eta*U, -eta*U),\n    G=(z, U, -eta*U, -eta*U, -eta*U),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (9)\n\nEvery GD update has the original step length. For these saturated\nvalues the original ordered interpolation residual is exactly\n\n    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.\n\nHere is a check of all twenty ordered finite pairs, grouped by their\ntarget j. Targets1 and4 have zero normal. At target0 the normal is\nU+a*z. The incoming pair(1,0) has residual0, whereas each incoming\npair(i,0), i=2,3,4, has residual-a*nu. At target2 the normal is\n-(b-eta)U. The only nonzero finite residual there is\nQ_12=-(1+eta)(b-eta). At target3 the normal is\n-(1+d)*eta*U. The only nonzero finite residual there is\nQ_13=-(1+eta)(1+d)*eta. Thus all twenty residuals are nonpositive.\nThe zero optimum jet can also be added: Q_(i,star)=0 by the chosen\nvalues; the opposite inequalities follow from the same normals and\na*eta<1. These optimum pairs are not additional multipliers in(1).\n\nFor completeness these are actual jets of one global smooth convex\nfunction. Let Kphys=conv{0,z,U,-eta*U} and define\n\n    F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).\n\nThe comparisons just made say that X_i-G_i is an outward normal\nto Kphys at G_i. Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer\nas the Euclidean projection of X onto Kphys. If projections at X,Y\nare G,H, adding their normal inequalities gives\n||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>, and hence ||G-H||<=||X-Y||.\nTo see differentiability directly, optimality at X and X+v bounds\nF(X+v)-F(X)-<G,v> between0 and<G(X+v)-G,v>, which is at most||v||^2.\nThus the gradient is the projection and is1-Lipschitz. The function\nis convex as a maximum of affine functions of X; it has minimum0 at0\nbecause0 belongs to Kphys. It realizes all of(9).\n\nBy(6), its primitive energy is\n\n    ||U+u*z||^2-C^2*eta^2\n      =a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2=Erow.            (10)\n\nLet\n\n    Bin=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40.\n\nIn the U coordinate the gradient column is-eta*e+(1+eta)*e1;\nin the V coordinate it is sqrt(nu)*e0. The constant kernel proved\nin Section2 therefore gives the full matrix remainder\n(1+eta)^2*M11+nu*m. Substituting every nonzero residual above into\nthe original identity(2) yields the exact accounting identity\n\n    Erow=nu*(m+a*Bin)+(1+eta)^2*M11\n       +(1+eta)*eta*((2+c+d)*Lambda12+(1+d)*Lambda13). (11)\n\nEvery term on the right is nonnegative. In particular\n\n    0<=m<=m+a*Bin<=Erow/nu.                              (12)\n\nNo incoming coefficient has been set to zero. Identity(11) prices the\noriginal incoming coefficients explicitly.\n\n### 5. The original first row couples the two energies\n\nDefine the weighted incoming combination\n\n    Bweight=(1+b)*Lambda20+(1-b*c)*Lambda30\n                                    +(1+b*c*d)*Lambda40.\n\nThe exact first-row identity is\n\n    2*e0^T M p = u*(m-delta)+a^2*Bweight.             (13)\n\nHere e0 denotes the original first coordinate column of R5. A direct\nderivation avoids any coordinate reduction. Since Tp=e and Le=0,\nMp=Dp+T^T L^T p/2. Also Te0=u*e-a*e0, giving\n\n    2*e0^T M p=2(1-a^2)-a*p^T L e0.\n\nThe flow at node0 gives Lambda10=Aout+2u-Bin. Hence\n\n    p^T L e0\n      =Aout+a*Lambda10-a*b*Lambda20\n                        +a*b*c*Lambda30-a*b*c*d*Lambda40\n      =u*Aout+2a*u-a*Bweight.\n\nFinally substitute a*Aout=1-a^2-m from(4). The constant term becomes\n\n    (2-u)(1-a^2)-2a^2*u\n      =u*((1-a)^2-2a^2)=-u*delta,\n\nwhich proves(13), with all row and flow contributions retained.\n\nAll three incoming multipliers are nonnegative. Since b,c,d>0,\neach coefficient in Bweight is at most Wrow. This remains true even\nif 1-b*c is negative. Thus Bweight<=Wrow*Bin. Applying(5) with e0,p,\nand using(8), gives from(13)\n\n    u*delta <= u*m+a^2*Wrow*Bin+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)\n             <= Krow*(m+a*Bin)+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)\n             <= Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).        (14)\n\nThis is(A). Put t=sqrt(Erow/nu). Because Krow,u,delta are positive,\n(14) says\n\n    Krow*t^2+2*sqrt(Qenergy)*t-u*delta>=0.\n\nSolving this increasing quadratic for t>=0 and rationalizing its\npositive root gives(B). Its numerator is strictly positive, so Erow>0.\nMultiplying(A) by nu and squaring only when its left side after\nsubtraction is positive gives exactly(C). There is no sign-losing\nsquaring in this formulation.\n\n### 6. A rational strengthened middle-cut boundary\n\nIf c*d<=1, then Wrow=1+b. If also a*b<=1, then\na*Wrow=a+a*b<=1+a=u, so Krow=u. Since Qenergy<1 and Erow>0,\n(14) implies the strict inequality\n\n    u*delta < u*t^2+2t,       t=sqrt(Erow/nu)>0.\n\nThe right side is strictly increasing for t>=0. Its value at\nt=delta/u is\n\n    delta^2/u+2delta/u=delta*(delta+2)/u=u*delta,\n\nbecause delta+2=u^2. It follows that t>delta/u, which is(D).\nThe exact algebraic identity\n\n    Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2\n      =[(a+3-delta*eta)^2-u^2*C^2*eta^2]/u^2          (15)\n\ncan be checked by expanding the constant, linear, and quadratic\ncoefficients in eta. Those coefficients in the numerator are\n(a+3)^2, -2(a+3)*delta, and delta^2-u^2*C^2.\n\nThe first factor a+3-delta*eta is positive: by a*eta<1,\n\n    a+3-delta*eta > a+3-delta/a = 1+1/a >0.\n\nThus(D) and(15) give\n\n    a+3 > (u*C+delta)*eta.\n\nSubstitute eta=b/(C-2-a-b), whose denominator is H>0. Since\ndelta+a+3=(a+1)(a+2), the result is exactly(E). Every denominator\nin(E) is positive.\n\nIn particular this is a necessary bound on an entire step region,\nnot a condition on a sparse coefficient family. The exact stronger\ncondition(A) still retains the actual Qenergy rather than discarding\nit in favor of the upper bound1.\n\n### 7. Adversarial scope checks\n\nThe initial-step hypothesis is substantive. At a=sqrt(2)-1 the\nquantity delta is zero, and(A) supplies no positive gap. This agrees\nwith the possibility of saturated basic certificates at that endpoint.\nWe do not extend the strict conclusion by continuity across delta=0.\n\nIf b*c>1, the coefficient of Lambda30 in(13) is negative. The proof\ndoes not replace this coefficient by its absolute value or infer a\nfalse lower bound. It uses only the valid upper bound Bweight<=Wrow*Bin.\nThus(A)--(C) still apply in that regime. The simplified statement(E)\nrequires only its explicitly stated a*b and c*d hypotheses.\n\nIf Qenergy=0, formula(B) reduces to Erow>=nu*u*delta/Krow;\nno division by Qenergy occurs. If a multiplier vanishes or M has\nadditional kernels, the semidefinite form of(5) still applies. No\nstrict complementarity, full rank, or strictly positive support was used.\n\nHere is a purely symbolic test family showing why the separate tests\nErow>=0 and Qenergy>=0 cannot justify the omitted coupling. It is an\nanalytical counterexample to that shortcut, not a numerical seed proposal\nor a claim to survive the other existing necessary inequalities. Take\n\n    a=1/2, eta=7/24, C=39*sqrt(2)/7,\n    H=24*(C-5/2)/31, b=7*(C-5/2)/31, Ktail=H-3,\n    0<d<min(Ktail/2,1/Ktail,1/(C*a*b*Ktail)), c=Ktail-d.\n\nThe elementary bounds 7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 give\n193/25<39/5<C<8. Thus Ktail>0, all four excesses are positive,\na*b<1, c*d<1, and 0<C*a*b*c*d<1. Also C=5+a+b+c+d\nand eta=b/(3+c+d). Direct exact substitution gives\n\n    R-k*eta=169/32=C^2*eta^2,\n    Erow=0, Qenergy>0,\n    nu=1271/576, delta=1/4, u=3/2.\n\nYet(D) requires Erow>1271/20736. Hence none of these words has the\nfull certificate. This illustrates the active original-row obstruction;\nit is not asserted to be a newly uncovered region beyond every prior\nobstruction.
